# Fluent UI React v9 parity plan

### Navigation native-root event contracts — Fluent 9.75.0 declarations and local Chromium, 2026-11-03

The installed Fluent Nav source declares a `div` root, NavItem an anchor root, and the category disclosure is a button. Local custom elements had direct React handlers on their hosts. Nav, NavItem, NavSubItem, AppItem, and NavCategoryItem now bridge click and capture callbacks to their native root controls; the Nav fixture checks callback target/currentTarget and compiles event types for those controls. The local Nav keeps its semantic `nav` root and NavItem uses a button when no destination exists, so its tag and types reflect that local semantic/polymorphic behavior rather than copying Fluent's element choice exactly.

### Generic shadow-root event target inference was rejected — local Card runtime, 2026-11-03

An experiment mapped React click handlers to the outermost element found in a custom element's composed event path. Card showed that this heuristic can select an implementation wrapper rather than the component's public root. The generic bridge was removed in `70b49ce`; native-root event bridging stays component-specific where the Fluent and local root contracts are known. Custom-element handlers without a verified native root remain open parity work.

`scripts/sync-component-catalog-signatures.mjs` now regenerates the catalog's first React parameter signatures from the TypeScript AST. The first parity gate after the event typing changes caught 11 stale signatures; regeneration updated them and `catalog:check-signatures` now confirms all 243 match. `parity:check` passes after the update. This automates only signature freshness; slot summaries and callback documentation remain separately maintained.

### Native-root React click events across action controls — Fluent 9.75.0 contracts and local Chromium, 2026-11-03

The custom-element host was receiving React click handlers for controls whose Fluent root slot is a native button, anchor, or span. Shadow retargeting therefore exposed the host as `currentTarget`, and host-level handlers could lose the native target shape. Added a shared bridge that resolves the action root from that component's own shadow tree and forwards both `onClick` and `onClickCapture` with the native event. Applied it to Button, MenuButton, CompoundButton, ToggleButton, Hamburger, and Link; Link's keyboard activation now clicks its internal span so the bridged native event follows the same path. Focused fixtures assert native target/currentTarget and callback behavior. Other components that forward inherited React event props still need a runtime audit; this is not full event or Fluent behavior parity.

### Native-root mouse, pointer, keyboard, and focus event contracts — React 18.3.1 and local Chromium, 2026-11-03

The component-specific bridge now maps click, keyboard, focus/blur, mouse, and pointer events to the verified native roots for Button, MenuButton, CompoundButton, ToggleButton, Hamburger, Link, Nav, NavItem, NavCategoryItem, NavSubItem, and AppItem. React 18.3.1 maps `focusin`/`focusout` to synthetic `focus`/`blur`; the bridge preserves that distinction and `relatedTarget`. The runtime tests cover key fields; capture/bubble order; all 8 simple MouseEvent types and 8 PointerEvent types on all 11 roots; pointer identity/pressure; and nested Nav ancestor propagation. Enter/leave use the exact native root, preserve React's synthetic type over native over/out events, and treat slotted descendants as inside the control. `scripts/verify-react-native-keyboard-events.mjs`, `scripts/verify-react-native-focus-events.mjs`, `scripts/verify-react-native-mouse-pointer-events.mjs`, and `scripts/verify-react-native-enter-leave-events.mjs` pass in local Chromium. Library build, generated signatures, the click regression suite, and `parity:check` pass. Drag, touch, wheel, form/change, animation, and other component families without a verified native-root bridge remain open parity work.

### SplitButton accessible name inherits the primary action — Fluent 9.75.0 source and local Chromium, 2026-11-03

The pinned Fluent `useSplitButtonBase_unstable` assigns the menu button an `aria-labelledby` reference to the primary action unless the consumer supplies an accessible name. The local control instead defaulted to a generic, localized “More options” label, including when callers supplied custom primary slots. SplitButton now names its menu action from the primary label or content, preserves an explicit menu-button name and the local `menuLabel` override, and keeps the localized provider string as a fallback. The shadow-root Button engine resolves `aria-labelledby` references from the owning document into the internal control's accessible name, since the referenced node is outside that control's shadow tree. The browser fixture mounts Fluent 9.75.0 beside the local implementation and compares the default names; it also covers React-element/object slots and a primary button labelled by an external element. `verify-button-parity` and provider localization checks pass with zero browser errors. Other controls still lack post-mount label-text observation; assistive technology, cross-browser, and authenticated tenant behavior remain open.

### SplitButton labels resolve after the full React commit — Fluent 9.75.0 and local Chromium, 2026-11-03

The first accessible-name fix read `aria-labelledby` targets during render. A target rendered later in the same React commit was not in the document yet, so both the primary and menu actions could retain the visible-text fallback. SplitButton now resolves the target in a layout effect after commit and synchronizes both native actions. It observes the referenced label elements for later text or naming-attribute changes; it watches the document only while a referenced ID is unresolved, then narrows observation to the label nodes. `verify-button-parity` places the referenced label after SplitButton in the same React fragment, changes its text after mount, and confirms both buttons update their accessible names; the focused verifier, library TypeScript build, and `git diff --check` pass. Other controls, assistive technology, cross-browser, and authenticated tenant behavior remain open.

### External `aria-labelledby` references across native shadow controls — Fluent contracts and local Chromium/WebKit, 2026-11-03

The shared React prop surface accepts native `aria-labelledby`, but an ID reference forwarded into an internal shadow root cannot resolve an element in the page tree. Added `syncHostAccessibleName` to resolve those document-level references into the internal control's accessible name while leaving assigned light-DOM native controls on their normal ID-reference path. Applied it to Button, Checkbox, ComboBox, Input, RadioGroup, SearchBox, Select, Slider, SpinButton, Switch, and TextArea. The existing form-associated-controls sweep now mounts all 18 controls with an external label and asserts the resolved name on each of those 11 native controls. Input parity also passes in Chromium and WebKit; Button/SplitButton and provider localization checks pass. See the dynamic-label checkpoint below; full accessible-name computation for nested references, assistive technology, and tenant behavior remain open.

### Dynamic external accessible-name synchronization — local Chromium, 2026-11-03

Static shadow-root name copying left controls stale when an external `aria-labelledby` target was added later, edited, or unlinked. The shared resolver now observes only referenced label elements (and watches the document only while a referenced ID is unresolved), restores the prior internal name when the host reference is removed, and disconnects observation when its host is detached. MenuButton now uses the same shadow-control resolver. The form-control fixture verifies late target insertion, target-text changes, and label removal across Button, Checkbox, ComboBox, Input, RadioGroup, SearchBox, Select, Slider, SpinButton, Switch, and TextArea; the Button fixture checks same-commit resolution plus dynamic SplitButton and MenuButton names. Both focused verifiers pass in Chromium, and the form-control mutation sweep also passes in WebKit. The library build and parity gate pass; the 51-family axe fixture reports zero violations and zero page errors. Nested accessible-name computation, replacement/removal of an already-resolved target node, assistive technology, and tenant behavior remain open.

### MessageBar group, announcement, and auto-reflow semantics — Fluent 9.75.0 runtime and local Chromium, 2026-11-03

The source audit showed that Fluent MessageBar is a named group linked to its title, and announces body/action changes through a politeness-aware live region; local code had instead changed the root role to `status`/`alert` and did not announce updates. Local MessageBar now preserves group semantics, links an available title unless the author supplied a label, and updates a shadow-root live region for body/action text changes. Its `layout="auto"` measures available/intrinsic width with the owning document's ResizeObserver and reflows between singleline and multiline, including authored layout changes and content updates. MessageBarGroup now retains removed bars as inert, aria-hidden visual clones for a Fluent-duration fade, then removes them; entry/exit animations honor reduced motion. The focused browser fixture mounts Fluent MessageBar beside the local control, compares group/title semantics and narrow/wide reflow, checks announcement updates and exit removal, forced colors, reduced motion, RTL and native composition, and reports zero page errors. `verify:message-bar-composition` passes. Cross-browser/screen-reader/tenant/theme/performance parity remain open.

### Skeleton context and progress semantics — Fluent 9.75.0 runtime and local Chromium, 2026-11-03

The Fluent source audit found two runtime gaps missed by the named-export and prop checks: there was no `SkeletonContextProvider`/`useSkeletonContext`, and standalone Skeleton/SkeletonItem semantics differed. Added both React context exports; SkeletonItem now inherits animation, appearance, size, and shape from context with explicit item props taking precedence. Skeleton supplies Fluent's `role="progressbar"` and `aria-busy="true"` defaults, while SkeletonItem remains a generic div. Numeric Skeleton width/height values now resolve to CSS pixels. The local Chromium fixture mounts Fluent's provider, Skeleton, and SkeletonItem beside the local implementation and checks context values, overrides, computed geometry, accessible attributes, native custom-element composition, and zero page errors. `verify:skeleton-item-parity`, `parity:check`, the library build, and the docs build pass. Full cross-browser theme/motion, screen-reader, tenant, and family performance parity remain open.

### SpinButton keyboard defaults and Enter submission — Fluent 9.75.0 runtime and local Chromium, 2026-11-03

Fluent only prevents the browser default for PageUp/PageDown. The local SpinButton had also canceled ArrowUp/ArrowDown, Home/End, and Enter, suppressed modified key combinations, and let Shift+Home/End change the bound. It now matches Fluent's default-prevention and Shift behavior. Enter commits and submits the associated form, activating its default submitter when the internal shadow input itself has no form owner; assigned inputs retain native submission. The verifier mounts the pinned Fluent SpinButton beside the local control, compares key default-prevention, confirms both forms submit on Enter, and checks shadow-input form submission. `verify:spin-button-parity` passes with zero page errors. Remaining cross-browser, assistive-technology, theme, tenant, and family performance parity are open.

The extended control audit caught two stepper differences: Fluent names fallback buttons “Increment value” and “Decrement value,” and begins stepping on press with an accelerating repeat while held. Framework-neutral and React-provider defaults now match; caller-supplied labels still override them. Held mouse presses use Fluent's initial delay and acceleration curve, and keyboard-generated clicks remain supported. The focused verifier compares both React default names and repeat cadence against pinned Fluent. Cross-browser, assistive-technology, theme, tenant, and family performance parity remain open.

### SearchBox native constraint validation — Fluent 9.75.0 input behavior and local Chromium, 2026-11-03

The SearchBox custom element forwarded native constraints such as `minlength` and `pattern` to its shadow input but only reflected `required` through `ElementInternals`. The enclosing form therefore missed native `tooShort` and `patternMismatch` states. It now maps the shadow input's `ValidityState` flags and validation message to the form-associated host. `verify:search-box-parity` confirms short and pattern-mismatched values fail the associated form and valid text restores `:valid`; it is now included in the form-controls sweep. The full `parity:check` also passes. Cross-browser, assistive-technology, tenant, and SearchBox-wide visual/performance parity remain open.

### ComboBox prefix activation and stale-selection clearing — Fluent 9.75.0 declarations/runtime and local Chromium, 2026-11-03

The input handler previously reset active-option state on every edit and retained a selected value when typed text no longer matched any enabled option. It now activates the first enabled prefix match, emits the active-option change, and clears a stale single selection on empty/unmatched input while preserving the typed text and emitting the selection callback. `verify:combobox-compounds`, `verify:combobox-slots`, and `verify:combobox-multiselect` pass; `parity:check` passes with 243 catalog entries, all 51 Fluent named-API families represented, and no candidate own-prop, slot, literal, or callback-shape gaps. This focused improvement does not establish full ComboBox family, cross-browser, assistive-technology, theme, tenant, or performance parity.

### TextArea replacement-slot styling — pinned Fluent 9.75.0 / local Chromium and SPFx 1.24 build, 2026-11-02

The replaceable native textarea slot did not receive `filled-darker-shadow` or `filled-lighter-shadow` styling because the shadow selector matched only the internal fallback control. Added the same theme-derived shadow to assigned textarea slots. The same audit found that host and disabled-fieldset updates could overwrite `disabled`, `required`, and `readOnly` values supplied by assigned native controls in both Input and TextArea. Both custom elements now retain assigned-control defaults and reapply them alongside host/fieldset state. Existing Chromium verifiers cover replacement-slot appearance, host prop changes, disabled-fieldset re-enable, controlled values, native attributes, sizing, form lifecycle, and callbacks; both pass with zero page errors. The SPFx 1.24 production package build and artifact measurement pass. Current tree-shaken Input slices: custom element 3,516 gzip / 3,062 Brotli bytes; React adapter 11,184 / 9,836 bytes. TextArea: 2,800 / 2,396 and 10,383 / 9,098 bytes respectively, with six React slice modules. The current SPFx 1.24 package (version 0.0.42) is 250,242 bytes; its demo bundle is 1,073,415 raw, 245,527 gzip, and 184,977 Brotli bytes. These package measurements include all current library changes and do not isolate either control fix; assistive-technology, cross-browser, full theme, and tenant behavior remain open.

### Slider orientation accessibility — Fluent 9.75.0 / react-slider 9.6.8, 2026-11-02

Fluent sets `aria-orientation="horizontal"` by default and updates the primary input's default orientation when `vertical` changes. The local custom element omitted horizontal orientation and removed the attribute when returning from vertical mode. It now supplies the default on the native range input, updates its own default when vertical changes, and retains an explicit orientation supplied through the input slot or host ARIA prop. The existing Chromium verifier covers horizontal default, vertical toggling in both directions, explicit slot and host overrides, and zero page errors; `verify:slider-parity` passes. Current tree-shaken Slider slices: custom element 3,050 gzip / 2,630 Brotli bytes; React adapter 10,644 / 9,337 bytes. This does not establish full slider keyboard, theme, cross-browser, assistive-technology, tenant, or family performance parity.

### Popover PositioningShorthand contract — Fluent 9.75.0 suite / react-positioning 9.23.4 declarations and local Chromium, 2026-11-02

Popover's React and custom-element paths previously had only partial positioning support. Added Fluent-compatible `PositioningShorthand` typing and runtime behavior for offset callbacks, `"width"` target matching, `positioningRef.updatePosition/setTarget`, `onPositioningEnd` and the `fui-positioningend` DOM event, `disableUpdateOnResize`, `shiftToCoverTarget`, and default transform placement. Absolute positioning now accounts for page scroll, and unmounted positioning refs are cleared. A TypeScript contract check proves all Fluent positioning shorthand values and option objects are accepted locally. Focused Chromium coverage verifies virtual target offsets and dimensions, ref retargeting/manual updates, resize observation controls, boundary covering, and transform placement; the broader Popover positioning, trigger, focus, appearance, context-menu, hover, and inline suites pass with zero page errors. Marked all 157 React.forwardRef wrappers as pure so bundlers can remove adapters that aren't imported. A measured Popover React slice dropped from 186,806 to 16,181 gzip bytes (91.3%); the 6 retained modules include shared overlay/theme code. This is a minified local bundle measurement, not tenant or page-load performance. Cross-browser, assistive-technology, SharePoint tenant, and family performance parity remain open.

### Option forwarded host ref — React adapter browser verification, 2026-11-02

The 157-host ref verifier caught that standalone `Option` passed its public ref directly to the rendered `div`, while `useImperativeHandle` was publishing a separate internal ref that remained null. The standalone listbox branch now assigns the rendered host to the internal ref; the shared bridge publishes that same host, while the Combobox branch continues to expose its custom-element option. `verify:react-refs` now passes for all 157 adapters, including both Option host modes. This local React/Chromium contract check does not replace assistive-technology or tenant validation.

### Tooltip PositioningShorthand contract — Fluent 9.75.0 declarations and local Chromium, 2026-11-02

Tooltip now accepts the Fluent positioning shorthand and object contract, including logical alignment, fallback placements, boundaries, auto sizing, target matching, offset callbacks, virtual targets, update refs, strategy, transforms, and positioning-end events. The React adapter forwards object positioning options to the custom element; placement and offset behavior are checked in Chromium. The type-contract fixture verifies every Fluent shorthand union branch is accepted locally. `verify:tooltip-positioning`, `verify:tooltip-parity`, `parity:check`, and the SPFx 1.24 production package build pass. The positioning verifier confirms collision handling, RTL, clipping/top-layer behavior, resizing, virtual-target retargeting, touch behavior, and no page errors. This closes the public Tooltip positioning contract gap; exhaustive Floating UI modifier semantics, assistive technology, cross-browser, and SharePoint tenant behavior remain unverified.

### Color picker React callback parity — pinned Fluent color-picker declarations and local Chromium, 2026-11-02

The form-control sweep found that ColorPicker, ColorArea, ColorSlider, and AlphaSlider exposed callback event types that did not match the installed Fluent color-picker declarations. Their previous shared signature also gave ColorSlider callbacks the wrong first-event type. Split the area and slider callback payloads, model Fluent's `EventData` tagged branches, and bridge range `input` events into React-shaped `change` events with the originating input as `currentTarget` and the native event retained as `nativeEvent`. The focused type fixture now checks two-way callback-type assignability against Fluent's declarations, and the Chromium verifier checks event tags, identity, current target, native event, HSV changes, RTL, form behavior, and composition. `verify:color-picker`, the full `verify:form-controls` sweep, and `parity:check` pass. This is local Chromium and type evidence; cross-browser, assistive-technology, and current-tenant validation remain open.

### Catalog-wide verifier reference gate — local static inventory, 2026-11-02

Added `npm run parity:runtime-coverage` and included its `--check` form in `parity:check`. The inventory scans all 243 catalog entries against verifier imports, explicit custom-element tags, generated typography-preset imports, and native module references; each entry now has a direct verifier-file reference. This prevents catalog additions from silently having no linked verifier, but is only a coverage index: a reference is not proof that all states, browsers, or accessibility behavior are tested. It exposed missing direct verifier references for CardFooter, CardPreview, MenuDivider, MenuGroup, MenuGroupHeader, and NavSectionHeader, which received focused React/native coverage in the same pass.

### Fluent structural semantics for Menu and Nav primitives — pinned local Fluent packages, Chromium

Pinned Fluent source comparison found `MenuGroup` should be named by `MenuGroupHeader`, `MenuDivider` is decorative (`role="presentation"`, `aria-hidden`), and `NavSectionHeader` renders as an h3. React and framework-neutral MenuGroups now associate their heading IDs, with the existing label retained as a fallback; dividers are hidden from assistive technology by default; and section headers expose heading level 3 while honoring explicit role/level overrides. `verify:menu-structural-components`, the Menu parity/submenu suites, Nav compound suite, `verify:card-primitives`, the catalog-wide parity gate, and the SPFx 1.24 production package build pass. Validation is local Chromium and does not replace browser/assistive-technology testing.

### React event bridge shape for form controls — local Chromium and SPFx 1.24 build, 2026-11-02

Input, Select, Slider, and SearchBox declared Fluent-shaped React event callbacks but forwarded native DOM events through type casts; RadioGroup separately built a partial bridge. Added one shared event bridge that retains the source browser event as `nativeEvent`, supplies the native control as `currentTarget`, and forwards default-prevention and propagation methods to both source and custom events. Mouse events also expose their React mouse-event fields. The focused Input, Select, Slider, and SearchBox Chromium verifiers, full static `parity:check`, and SPFx 1.24 production build/package-solution pass. This fixes the known event-object shape mismatch; it does not make the bridge an actual React-created SyntheticEvent or establish full callback semantics across all components, browsers, assistive technologies, or SharePoint tenant rendering.

### DataGrid subtle selection in composed React grids — local Chromium, 2026-11-02

Fluent's `DataGrid` context carries `subtleSelection`, and each `DataGridSelectionCell` inherits it. The local prop already worked in virtual React and native grids but was discarded by composed React grids. Added it to `DataGridCompositionProps` and context; selection cells inherit the grid setting while an explicit cell-level value can override it. Hover and focus now reveal the actual custom selection indicator as well as the native input, and the animation respects reduced motion and preserves checked/mixed visibility. The composition verifier checks hidden and revealed indicator opacity; the full DataGrid group passes, including async source loading and RTL keyboard navigation.

### DataGrid context selector and Fluent table-state projection — local Chromium, 2026-11-02

The composed DataGrid exposed its context provider but no Fluent-shaped selector hook. Added `DataGridContextValue<T>` and `useDataGridContext_unstable(selector)` over the library's own composition context, backed by `useSyncExternalStore`; a memoized consumer updates when its selected value changes and skips context-driven renders when another field changes. `DataGridContextValue<T>` now structurally extends Fluent's `TableFeaturesState<T>` and adds SharePoint-specific state. The context projects live table helpers: `getRows`, row selection/actions, sorting, `tableRef`, column sizing and keyboard mode, `selectableRows`, and the composite-row Tabster attribute. These projections update the actual composed grid; indexed sources expose loaded rows and preserve query-scoped all-row selection. Outside a provider, the hook supplies empty table state and safe defaults. Updated `DataGridContextProvider`'s public value type, row render helper context, catalog signature, and agent guide. The focused Chromium verifier compiles the structural type contract and covers defaults, render isolation, selection/sort/resize state, refs, row-navigation metadata, and indexed-source selection; `verify:data-grid-parity` and `parity:check` pass. Full table-context behavior under SharePoint and assistive technology remains unverified.

### Toast default behavior — pinned Fluent 9.75.0, local Chromium, 2026-11-02

Fluent declares a 3,000 ms default timeout and disables hover/window-blur pausing by default. The React and custom-element implementations defaulted to 5,000 ms and paused for both conditions. Aligned React, native `Toast`, and `Toaster.show` defaults; direct custom elements without pause attributes now also keep timing. `verify:toast-parity` asserts both adapters' default attributes and expiration while hovered and after a window blur event, alongside the existing queue and controller cases. The default behavior now matches the pinned declaration; focus-in-region pauses remain an intentional additional interaction behavior.

### Input React change-event contract — pinned Fluent 9.75.0, local Chromium, 2026-11-02

Fluent types `Input.onChange` as `React.ChangeEvent<HTMLInputElement>`. The local adapter exposed a generic DOM `Event` even though it forwards the originating native input event. Updated the public callback type and forwards that source event, matching the existing Slider adapter bridge. `verify:input-parity` now compiles callback use of `currentTarget.value`/`checked` and checks the runtime current target during dispatch, in addition to the existing control/form checks. This validates the typed contract and current synchronous input dispatch path; the event object is the underlying native event rather than a React-created SyntheticEvent instance.

### Table sort setter column IDs — pinned Fluent 9.19.23, local type fixture, 2026-11-02

Fluent's `TableSortState.setColumnSort` and `toggleColumnSort` require a concrete `TableColumnId`; local signatures also allowed `undefined`. Narrowed the public and plugin callback signatures to match Fluent while retaining `undefined` only for the current `sortColumn` state. `verify:table-hooks` now includes `@ts-expect-error` consumer calls for both invalid setter arguments; the browser suite continues to cover actual sort behavior.

The async keyboard case in the grouped run then exposed a separate row-height mismatch: DataGrid virtual spacers used the configured 32px height while composed rows/cells stayed at 40px, causing scroll anchoring to oscillate across adjacent load ranges and abort both requests. Composed row, data-cell, and selection-cell minimums now inherit `rowHeight`. Keyboard focus waits for a virtualized destination to mount (up to five seconds, or until a source error) before focusing it. The 2,001-row async source fixture now passes row navigation, bounded rendering, load-error retry, and page-error checks; the scenario's failure output includes mounted rows, cached indices, and recent request outcomes for faster diagnosis.

### Toast controller document routing — pinned Fluent 9.75.0, local Chromium, 2026-11-02

Fluent's `useToastController` dispatches document-level events, so the hook works outside a `Toaster` subtree and silently waits for a matching Toaster. The local hook previously required context or an already-mounted named Toaster and threw otherwise. Added document routing for unscoped and named external controllers; mounted Toasters handle dispatch, both update forms, dismiss, dismiss-all, pause/play, and focus commands while filtering by `toasterId`. Existing in-subtree and named registry paths remain direct. `npm run verify:toast-parity`, `npm run parity:check`, and `npm run build:library` pass. Browser coverage is local Chromium; cross-document portal and assistive-technology behavior remain unverified.

### Table composite navigation RTL fallback — local Chromium, 2026-11-02

The installed Tabster mover swaps horizontal arrow keys when the active direction is RTL, but the local no-Tabster fallback moved by DOM index without that direction mapping. The fallback now advances to the next logical cell on ArrowLeft in RTL and moves backward on ArrowRight; row entry retains Fluent's current ArrowRight behavior. Added an RTL browser fixture alongside the existing LTR checks. `npm run verify:table-hooks` and `npm run parity:check` pass. This validates the local fallback in Chromium; it does not replace authenticated SharePoint or screen-reader validation.

### Fluent table utility types and React contexts — local Chromium, 2026-11-02

Comparing `@fluentui/react-table` 9.19.23 declarations exposed a generic mismatch: local `TableFeaturePlugin<TItem>` fixed the row type before `useTableFeatures` could infer it. Aligned it with Fluent's generic plugin contract and mutable `TableFeaturesState` arrays. The same inventory found eight missing exports across Fluent's table, header, row-ID, and column-ID contexts; added the providers/hooks and made local `Table`/`TableHeader` supply table/header context to descendants. Catalog now records and validates Table's public helpers. The in-memory TypeScript consumer fixture verifies generic plugin composition and context types; Chromium verifies automatic and explicit context propagation alongside existing selection, sort, sizing, and keyboard behavior. Library build, `verify:table-hooks`, catalog validation, and `parity:check` pass. Lower-level unstable Fluent table render/style hooks and full assistive-technology/RTL behavior remain separate parity work.

### Toast controller utility contract — local Chromium, 2026-11-02

Comparison with Fluent 9.75.0 `useToastController` found that the local hook only reached the nearest `Toaster` and lacked Fluent's `updateToast({ toastId, content, ...options })` form. Added optional `toasterId` registration/routing so a hook elsewhere in the React tree can target a named Toaster, and kept the existing nearest-Toaster and two-argument update forms. Toast IDs now accept Fluent's `toastId` alias alongside the existing `id`. The Toast browser suite verifies out-of-subtree dispatch, object-form content/priority update, dismissal, prior two-argument updates, queue behavior, timers, keyboard management, and no page errors. TypeScript build, catalog validation, and `parity:check` pass. This closes the identified controller slice; global default-toaster behavior and broader Fluent toast runtime parity remain distinct questions.

### Fluent icon/wrapper slot and SearchBox appearance slice — local Chromium, 2026-11-02

Resolved the Fluent slot audit across imported declaration aliases; it now checks 240 secondary-slot contracts rather than silently skipping them. Added missing React `icon`/`wrapper` slot projections and updated catalog signatures/slot metadata. Added SearchBox `filled-darker-shadow` and `filled-lighter-shadow` appearances, plus a browser regression check for both. The combined parity gate reports zero named API family gaps, own-prop gaps, literal gaps, slot gaps, callback-field gaps, or sampled native-prop gaps; the React adapter build and catalog validation pass. Chromium checks pass for the new slot assignments and SearchBox appearance styles, plus the existing Breadcrumb, Carousel, Toolbar, and Nav suites with no browser errors. The audits compare declared contracts and the reported component suites test selected behavior; they do not prove complete runtime, accessibility, theme, assistive-technology, cross-browser, tenant, or performance parity.

### Inherited React prop coverage and SearchBox attributes — local Chromium, 2026-11-02

The native-slot prop audit covered only nine families, so it missed the input-attribute contract inherited by Fluent SearchBox and didn’t mechanically sample Checkbox, Switch, RadioGroup, Slider, SpinButton, ComboBox, Dropdown, ColorPicker, or Rating. Expanded the audit to 19 comparable component families, documenting intentional domain-specific overrides and SearchBox's inherited input surface. SearchBox now derives its React props from the existing Input contract (excluding unsupported children, the fixed `type`, and its separately managed input slot) and forwards `autoComplete`, `maxLength`, `minLength`, `pattern`, and `inputMode` into the native search field, including dynamic host-attribute updates. The Chromium verifier checks those properties and their updates; `parity:native-props` now reports zero candidates across all 19 families. Component-specific props, native event routing, and non-React usage still require their own audits; this check is not proof of full Fluent runtime parity.

### Form ownership parity across React controls — local Chromium, 2026-11-02

The external-form association check found 13 React control APIs missing the native `form` prop type even though their custom-element hosts already accepted the attribute. Added it to Checkbox, ColorPicker, ComboBox, DatePicker, Dropdown, FileUpload, RadioGroup, Rating, SearchBox, Slider, SpinButton, Switch, and TagPicker. The check also showed that several hosts relied on `ElementInternals.form` internally but did not expose the native-like `host.form` getter; aligned that getter across the form-associated families, including Select, Button, and the existing Input, TextArea, RadioGroup, Toggle, and RichTextEditor implementations. Added `verify:form-associated-controls` to `parity:check`; it checks form prop types through the TypeScript checker and mounts all 18 React form-control APIs outside their target form in Chromium. The focused check, full form-control verification suite, `parity:check`, and `git diff --check` pass. Form lifecycle checks remain Chromium-only; Firefox, WebKit, SharePoint tenant, and assistive-technology coverage remain open.

### RichTextEditor native validity and localized required message — local Chromium, 2026-11-02

Input and TextArea expose their form-associated validity API, but RichTextEditor did not expose `form`, `validity`, `validationMessage`, `willValidate`, `checkValidity()`, `reportValidity()`, or `setCustomValidity()`, despite already participating in forms. Added those host APIs, preserving required and custom-error flags, barring disabled/read-only editors from constraint validation, and restoring validation when a disabled fieldset is re-enabled. Routed the provider's new `richTextRequired` message through the `required-message` attribute. The browser-state restoration hook is now exposed and handles restored string values like Input and TextArea. The Arabic browser verifier covers validity state/message, custom errors, dynamic message updates, disabled/read-only handling, form submission/reset, restored values, and zero page errors. Full rich-text and static parity checks pass; cross-browser validation remains open.
React `RichTextEditorProps` also now accepts the native `form` association attribute. The verifier confirms a React editor outside a form's DOM subtree associates by ID and contributes its named HTML to that form's `FormData`.

### SwatchPicker provider localization — local Chromium, 2026-11-02

React `SwatchPicker` had no `aria-label` fallback from `SharePointUIProvider`, and `EmptySwatch` retained an English accessible name even inside a localized subtree. The picker now uses the existing `colorSwatches` string when no explicit label is supplied; the add-color control uses new optional `colorAdd` text, with an English default. The focused Arabic browser verifier confirms both host and internal button names; explicit user labels continue to take precedence. `verify:swatch-picker`, `parity:check`, and `git diff --check` pass. Other generated labels and provider-free custom-element defaults still need a systematic inventory.

### RichTextEditor image-picker localization — local Chromium, 2026-11-02

The React toolbar's image insertion button already used provider strings, but its hidden file input retained the English accessible name `Choose image`. Added optional `richTextChooseImage` provider text and an `image-input-label` custom-element attribute; the native engine updates the input label both on first render and when the attribute changes. The Arabic RichTextEditor verifier checks the localized name and a live language update. This closes that editor label gap; localization inventory for all components and browser/tenant coverage remain open.

### Inherited native prop parity pilot — local Chromium, 2026-11-02

The own-prop parity report excludes inherited React/DOM props, so its clean result hid actionable gaps. Added `scripts/report-native-prop-gaps.mjs`, a TypeScript-checker-based contract audit for the native primary elements of Button, Card, Text, Badge, Avatar, Input, Link, Select, and TextArea. It compares names, optionality, and native-to-local value assignability; event routing and runtime semantics remain outside its scope. The audit now reports zero type-contract candidates across these nine families and runs as part of `parity:check`. The Card, Text, Badge, and Avatar browser verifiers additionally assert runtime `className` and inherited `title` forwarding. This is still a limited contract sample, not proof of all Fluent behavior parity.

Select now forwards `autoComplete`, external `form`, and `multiple`; React scalar/array values and defaults work, including repeated `FormData` values and reset. Browser tests caught and fixed a present `multiple="false"` attribute and lost React option-selection state. Link now forwards `hrefLang`, `media`, `ping`, and `type`. TextArea forwards native form, length, direction, width, autocomplete, and wrapping attributes. Button copies submit override attributes onto its form submitter. Input now exposes the inherited native prop names and supports number/array values plus checked/default-checked form participation. `verify:select-parity`, `verify:textarea-parity`, `verify:link-parity`, the Button polymorphic form verifier, and `verify:input-parity` cover these slices. These focused browser checks plus the native prop type audit improve the evidence, but do not establish every component's event routing or runtime parity.

### DataGrid provider localization coverage — local Chromium, 2026-11-02

The React DataGrid hard-coded default English labels for its grid name, filter, row/select-all controls, loading and empty states, sort priority, resize/reorder controls, group states, and edit hint, in addition to live-region clipboard and reorder messages. Added `SharePointUIProvider` string keys for these labels and templated statuses, applied them to both the virtual and composable React grids, and retained explicit `label`/`aria-label` overrides. The Lithuanian browser fixture verifies grid, filter, row and select-all checkbox, reorder button, resize separator, and clipboard success/failure names. `verify:provider-label-localization`, `verify:data-grid-clipboard`, `verify:data-grid-parity`, `parity:check`, and `git diff --check` pass, including async composed-grid ARIA label regression coverage. Other hard-coded text across the catalog and provider-free native elements still needs inventory and localization review.

### Combobox open-change transition and source-event parity — local Chromium, 2026-11-02

The custom element included source events for explicit open/close calls, but ComboBox and Dropdown adapters discarded them; additionally, typing and Arrow navigation mutated the engine's `open` state without emitting `spui-open-change`. Both React adapters now forward the original source event to `onOpenChange` and provider `setOpen`, while programmatic transitions retain the custom-event fallback. The engine emits one open-change notification for pointer, typing, Arrow navigation, Escape, blur, and imperative state changes. The compound verifier asserts click, keydown, blur, and programmatic callback paths. `verify:combobox-compounds`, `verify:combobox-multiselect`, `parity:check`, and `git diff --check` pass. React's local callback contract uses native DOM `Event`; exact Fluent SyntheticEvent compatibility and cross-browser/tenant behavior remain unverified.

### ComboboxProvider context contract — local Chromium, 2026-11-02

Review against the installed Fluent 9.75.0 `@fluentui/react-combobox` source found that the local public `ComboboxProvider` accepted only an app-specific `{ value, selectedOptions, open, callbacks }` object. Fluent's provider value also includes `registerOption`, `selectOption`, `setActiveOption`, `setOpen`, `activeOption`, `focusVisible`, `appearance`, and `size`. Added the exported Fluent-shaped `ComboboxContextValue` and `ComboboxOptionValue` types, widened `ComboboxProviderValue` compatibly, and wired the shared Dropdown/ComboBox path to register options and forward selection, active-option, value, and open updates. Provider-controlled open state now owns the transition instead of being immediately reset to its stale value by the component event handler. `verify:combobox-compounds` covers registration, provider appearance/size, controlled opening, selecting an option, active-option updates, and closing. `verify:combobox-multiselect`, `parity:check`, and `git diff --check` pass. Fluent's context itself is deprecated/unstable; this is compatibility for the public provider surface, not a dependency on Fluent runtime internals.

### Forced-colors, contrast, and mount benchmark checkpoint — local Chromium, 2026-11-02

`npm run verify:forced-colors` passes the bundled control, tooltip, dialog, popover, menu, alert, drawer, message-bar, toast, and teaching-popover verifiers with zero browser errors. `npm run verify:theme-contrast` passes 6,560 control-state readings across light/dark, five looks, four accents, light/strong surfaces, and LTR/RTL; minima are 4.56:1 for tested text, 3.84:1 for focused input borders, 4.96:1 for checked checkbox indicators, 5.52:1 for tags, and 3.77:1 for progress fills. Nav adds 160 label and 80 focus readings (minimum 11.09:1 and 7.89:1); Rating adds 3,840 star readings across 240 cases (minimum 4.39:1). The current 500-button custom-theme mount benchmark measured SharePoint UI React at 9.1 ms median / 10.3 ms p95 and Fluent React at 8.6 / 9.6 ms; 100-button default-theme medians were 1.8 ms for both. A CPU profile attributes only 1.7 ms total across 500 SPUI custom-element renders and points mostly to DOM/layout work, so no speculative component rewrite was made. The remaining 0.5 ms large-batch custom-theme median gap needs a targeted rendering/layout experiment; this synthetic local Chromium benchmark is not a tenant or cross-browser latency result.

### SharePoint theme inheritance measurement correction — published Lab 059, 2026-11-02

The previous 8-host warning was an audit false positive: it required raw theme variables on hidden Radio/ComboBox option descriptors and on nested Avatar hosts whose visible surface lives inside another component's shadow tree. The SharePoint diagnostic now samples the rendered radio input, open combobox option, and avatar-group child avatar and compares their semantic accent and raw SharePoint brand token with the active page values. Six option descriptors without their own raw token are excluded from the visual-surface failure count. Static audits also pass for all 243 catalog entries, 0 candidate prop/slot gaps, 0 callback-field gaps, 29 shared theme/layer roles used across 67 source files, and SharePoint effects/spacing/font tokens. This corrects the theme metric; it does not establish full behavioral, accessibility, or performance parity. The app instance remains status 9 with no registered manifest; the published page is using the current bundle, and two SharePoint host-script promise exceptions remain unattributed.

### DataGrid React edit-loop fix and ordered-column hot path — local Chromium, 2026-11-02

The React DataGrid edit verifier exposed a repeatable unmount after a successful edit. Captured browser stacks traced “Maximum update depth exceeded” to the column-window layout effect calling its state updater when horizontal virtualization was disabled. The grid now skips that unused window update, and its default empty width/sizing options are shared objects so ordinary rerenders do not invalidate memo dependencies. The custom-element column-order getter also returns the original definitions for default order and uses a `Set` for explicit order membership. The verifier now emits a non-waiting DOM/error snapshot on failure. `verify-data-grid-edit` passes React and native keyboard editing, validation, text/number commit, async save rejection, and Escape cancellation; composition, async source, focus mode, sizing, clipboard, horizontal virtualization, TypeScript build, and static parity checks pass.

The current 5,000-row local Chromium benchmark reports 11–12 mounted rows and 154–168 cells with 20 or 100 columns. With 100 columns, mount/layout is 5.2 ms median / 6.0 ms p95 and visible repaint is 3.7 / 4.4 ms; scroll paint is 19.0 / 19.9 ms including the next animation frame. Repeated local runs vary by roughly 1–2 ms, so the getter change has no proven timing win yet; the reliable gains are avoiding unused horizontal-window state work and avoiding quadratic membership checks for explicitly ordered columns. This is not a Fluent comparison or SharePoint-host benchmark. The open tenant package registration issue and assistive-technology review remain outstanding.

### List action-only keyboard accessibility — local Chromium, 2026-11-02

Reviewing List against the installed Fluent v9.75.0 `@fluentui/react-list` source exposed an accessibility gap beyond the currently tested selection modes: a `ListItem` with `onAction` in a plain List had `tabIndex=-1`, making its Enter/Space action unreachable by keyboard. Action rows now receive a Tab stop when selection/navigation mode is not managing a roving tab order. Fixed a separate Home/End edge case: End used a forward skip direction and failed to land on the last enabled row when a trailing row was disabled. The test now checks an action-only row by Tab focus, Enter, and Space, and verifies End and ArrowUp skip the disabled-selection row. `npm run verify:list` passes with the targeted axe audit and no page errors. `npm run verify:a11y-tree-accordion` passes its 51-family axe fixture with zero violations; its `incomplete` heuristic results (for example, obscured contrast sampling and `aria-controls` across shadow roots) are separately recorded and are not counted as proven issues. SPFx 1.24 packaging also passes and emits `library-demo-web-part_d7be8c6779464fd7c69e.js`; Heft reports zero Jest suites. These local runs do not replace screen-reader or tenant review.

### React root-ref inventory and mechanical forwarding pass — local Chromium, 2026-11-02

Expanded React host refs from 19 existing adapters to 156 of the 243 catalogued adapters. An AST codemod converted 86 single-host wrappers with one unambiguous custom-element root and no local ref; the pass also added refs to ten presentation/feedback roots by hand, then added refs to Checkbox and Switch through their shared `useToggle` event/state hook. A second AST codemod composed external refs with local event/state refs across 47 adapters using `useImperativeHandle`, and Tabs and Accordion received explicit generic-preserving ref wrappers. The browser verifier imports every catalog entry marked with a React ref type, mounts them in isolated error boundaries, and confirms each ref is a connected `HTMLElement` with the expected host tag. It exposed two conditional `Option`/`OptionGroup` branches that render plain `div` roots, so refs were added to those branches and the verifier accounts for their standalone Listbox behavior. The verifier supplies required values to Tab and TabPanel to avoid fixture-only runtime errors. Input and TextArea refs also reach native `select()` and preserve selection.

Added `npm run report:react-refs` for the recurring AST inventory, `npm run codemod:react-refs -- --write` for conservative conversion, and `npm run codemod:react-refs:compose -- --write` for local-ref composition. The codemods only change single-root adapters with a typed public signature and an unambiguous host/ref path. The catalog validator checks exact API signature text after the named `forwardRef` declaration, including generic adapters. SPFx 1.24 production packaging passes and emits `library-demo-web-part_b14fca1a185133f8baca.js`; Heft reports zero Jest suites in the sample project. This is package/build evidence, not a tenant deployment. Refs remain one parity dimension, not a claim that the component catalog has reached Fluent behavior/accessibility/theme parity.

### React host refs for Text, Link, and Card — local Chromium, 2026-11-02

Expanded the host-ref contract from Button/Input/TextArea to Text, Link, Card, Badge, Tag, and Avatar. Card and Tag preserve their internal event subscriptions while composing external refs with local host refs. The agent catalog records `reactRefType: HTMLElement`; its validator checks each `forwardRef` signature, keeping the agent-facing API index synchronized. `verify:react-refs` confirms all nine refs resolve to their exact custom-element hosts, and Input/TextArea still expose working `focus()`/`select()` methods. Card selection, Text presets, Badge, Tag, Avatar, TypeScript build, the full static Fluent API/prop/slot/callback audit, and SPFx production packaging pass. ### Provider-level theme propagation and themed mount benchmark — local Chromium, 2026-11-02

The themed Button benchmark exposed per-component token copying as a real React cost: 500 Buttons under a glass provider with 80 extra custom properties measured 49.8 ms median / 51.2 ms p95, against Fluent dark-theme Buttons at 7.9 / 9.6 ms. `SharePointUIProvider` now puts its merged theme map on one `display: contents` wrapper; custom-element hosts inherit through light DOM and shadow roots, while caller `style` props remain inline and take precedence. This avoids changing each component's token cascade and gives the provider-level shape used by Fluent. The benchmark asserts one provider root and zero child inline-style hosts; the sample root carries 187 style properties for this pack.

After the change, 500 themed SharePoint React Buttons measure 9.2 ms median / 10.6 ms p95 versus Fluent at 8.7 / 9.1 ms; 100 themed Buttons measure 2.0 / 2.5 ms versus 1.8 / 2.2 ms. Default SharePoint React at 500 measures 9.0 / 10.2 ms versus Fluent's 7.7 / 9.8 ms, while direct custom elements measure 6.3 / 6.9 ms. These are 20 local headless Chromium samples after five warmups, not cross-browser or SharePoint-host results. The full theme/forced-colors suite, 51-family axe fixture (zero violations), form-control suite, React motion/Rating checks, Button behavior suites, SharePoint token inheritance/override checks, static API parity audit, SPFx production package build, and diff check pass. Heft reports zero Jest suites in this example project; the TypeScript, Webpack, and production package steps succeed. Provider now adds a `display: contents` DOM wrapper: use it at the app/page root, outside slot-driven custom-element composition; README and agent guidance document this constraint. Tenant and assistive-technology validation remain outstanding.

### React host-style allocation and Button benchmark checkpoint — local Chromium, 2026-11-02

The default `useHostProps` path previously allocated an identical default style object for every React-hosted element. It now reuses one frozen object unless the caller supplies a style override. TypeScript build, the focused Button behavior/context/polymorphic suites, `parity:check`, and `git diff --check` pass. The 500-Button Fluent/SharePoint React/native benchmark measured medians of 8.2/9.8/6.4 ms and p95s of 9.5/10.9/6.7 ms (20 measured rounds after five warmups, local Chromium). This run does not show a latency win from the allocation change; it only removes repeated default style-object allocations. It also confirms the React adapter remains slower than Fluent in this workload while direct custom elements are faster. This single-process benchmark does not establish a cross-browser or SharePoint-host performance result.

### Button stylesheet reuse — local Chromium, 2026-11-02

The Button custom element now shares one constructable stylesheet per owner document instead of parsing and attaching a `<style>` element for each shadow root. `verify:button-parity` asserts that nine mounted Button roots share the same stylesheet, contain 31 Button rules, and create zero per-root style elements; the existing form, focus, polymorphic, slot, and activation checks also pass. Local 500-button mount samples moved from 10.6 ms median / 12.1 ms p95 to 9.9 ms / 10.7 ms; the measured custom-element render total moved from about 2.0 ms to 1.6 ms. These are noisy single-run headless Chromium samples, so the verified structural reuse is the firm result; the timing difference is modest and needs repeated cross-browser and tenant benchmarking before claiming a meaningful performance gain. Unsupported browsers retain the per-root `<style>` fallback. See `scripts/verify-button-parity.mjs` and the [benchmark ledger](component-slice-benchmarks.json).

### Expanded local accessibility fixture — 2026-11-02

The axe fixture renders representative compositions from all 51 Fluent families. Tightened the gate to fail on any axe violation, not only serious/critical findings. This found a serious AvatarGroup label-content mismatch: its overflow button displayed `+N` but its accessible name omitted that visible text. Direct contrast checks now cover all 19 axe-incomplete color targets, including both Combobox and Dropdown chevrons, the Rating glyphs, avatars, drawer, toast, and carousel cards; the lowest direct graphical contrast is 4.91:1. This is local Chromium coverage, not authenticated SharePoint or screen-reader validation.

### Rating and RatingDisplay contrast across themes — local Chromium, 2026-11-02

Manual contrast checks for axe-incomplete decorative graphics found the Rating empty-star fallback at 1.67:1 on white. Rating and RatingDisplay now derive empty stars from the active text/surface colors; neutral fills use the theme text color, and brand/marigold fills blend toward active text so they remain visible on light and strong surfaces. Added `npm run verify:rating-theme-contrast`: 3,840 filled/empty glyph readings across both components, three color modes, five looks, four brand colors, light and brand-paired strong surfaces, and LTR/RTL pass with a 4.39:1 minimum. The 51-family axe fixture now persists a full JSON report and prints a compact summary; it reports zero violations, and direct checks cover all 19 axe-incomplete color-contrast targets. The forced-colors suite now checks Rating and RatingDisplay stars against CanvasText/Highlight. Focused RatingDisplay behavior, 51-family axe, integrated theme contrast, and forced-colors suite pass. `npm run build:spfx` packages SPFx 1.24 successfully: `.sppkg` 262,353 bytes; demo bundle 1,136,993 raw / 257,603 gzip / 192,899 Brotli bytes. Tree-shaken custom-element slices are 2,944 gzip / 2,552 Brotli for Rating and 1,964 / 1,671 for RatingDisplay; React adapter measurements share the same 62-module bundle and are not incremental component costs. Heft reports zero Jest suites. This closes measured star contrast in those configured cases; broader component-state theme and assistive-technology coverage remains open.

**Reference baseline:** `@fluentui/react-components` 9.75.0, the version in the recently uploaded SPFx project. Parity means comparable user-facing capability, theme behavior, accessibility, composability, and documentation. It does not mean copying Fluent's internal React/Griffel architecture or promising drop-in API compatibility. The public core remains framework-independent custom elements, with a React adapter and use from other frameworks/plain HTML. Solutions that package `ClientSideAssets` install per site; set `skipFeatureDeployment` to false and include a feature upgrade action for existing sites. Before building more families, pilot existing Web Component libraries against the same SPFx and theming gates.

SharePointUIProvider now localizes default labels for Toolbar, Breadcrumb, MenuBar, SplitButton, Hamburger, SplitNavItem, VirtualList/Virtualizer (including both legacy scroll views), DataGridSelectionCell, and TableResizeHandle. Added optional string keys: `toolbarLabel`, `breadcrumbLabel`, `menuBarLabel`, `splitButtonMenuLabel`, `navigationToggle`, `splitNavItemActionLabel`, `virtualizerLabel`, `dataGridSelectRow`, and `tableResizeHandle`. Explicit component labels remain authoritative; Toolbar/MenuBar also honor explicit `aria-label` values. README, agent guidance, and catalog signatures are synchronized. The Lithuanian Chromium fixture covers provider defaults and overrides for each path with zero page errors. Library build, virtualizer scroll/axis checks, Table hooks, DataGrid React/native composition and async loading, 243-entry catalog, Fluent named API/prop/slot/literal/callback audits, 396-token drift check, and `git diff --check` pass. The virtualizer axis check failed one run at the RTL final-item visibility assertion, then passed on immediate rerun with the item inside the viewport; the failure path now prints item/viewport geometry. The post-change 51-family axe fixture passes with zero violations and zero page errors; shadow-local ARIA and contrast cases remain axe-incomplete and are not treated as proven by that run.

### Brand-matched strong-section and disabled-state contrast matrix — local Chromium, 2026-11-02

The contrast harness now measures 6,560 active and disabled control states across 160 combinations: light/dark color schemes, Fluent/iOS/Windows/Linux/glass looks, four accents (including tenant teal), light surfaces, brand-paired navy/green/plum/teal strong surfaces, and LTR/RTL. The expanded surfaces caught a tenant-teal strong-section Link at 4.39:1. The fallback link blend moved from 65% accent / 35% text to 55% / 45%; the full matrix now passes with minimum active text 4.56:1, Tag text 5.52:1, non-text contrast 3.84:1, and ProgressBar fill-to-track 3.77:1. Disabled Buttons, Input, and checked Checkbox are measured with opacity composited against the rendered section; their minimum is 1.57:1 for a disabled primary Button. These disabled readings are telemetry and are not treated as WCAG 1.4.3 failures. The verifier persists the full JSON report at `test-results/theme-contrast/results.json`. The separate forced-colors suite passes for representative controls (including 15 Tag variants), Tooltip, Dialog, Popover, nested Menu, Alert, Drawer, MessageBar, Toast, and TeachingPopover. Broader live tenant palettes, actual Windows contrast-theme validation, and a full component-by-component forced-colors matrix remain open.

### Measured Virtualizer anchor correction in RTL — local Chromium, 2026-11-02

A repeatable RTL axis failure exposed a runtime defect: after a distant scrollTo(4999), ResizeObserver refined visible item sizes and increased the measured scroll extent. The old anchor compensation ran before React committed the new extent, so the browser clamped the requested scroll and the final item shifted outside the viewport. The implementation now accumulates measured anchor corrections and applies them in a layout effect after the updated sizes commit. The axis verifier waits for the final row to be both visible and geometrically stable, and reports row/viewport bounds if it cannot settle. Three consecutive Chromium axis runs pass; each confirms bounded rendering, LTR and RTL target visibility, window scrolling, variable widths, native and React virtualizers, and zero page errors. Virtualizer scroll-view, observer/measurement utility, async range data-source, Fluent catalog/API, token, and whitespace checks also pass. This closes the measured RTL target-visibility defect; cross-browser and tenant scroll behavior remain unverified.

### Nav multiple-category default — local Chromium, 2026-11-02

Fluent 9.75 defaults `Nav.multiple` to `true`; this library had defaulted to a single open category in both React and direct custom-element use. The React adapter now defaults to multiple and emits an explicit `multiple="false"` when single-open is requested; the native engine treats an omitted attribute as multiple. `defaultOpenCategories` now respects single-open mode by opening only the first value. Focused Chromium checks pass for both adapters, initial multi/single category state, keyboard toggling, and zero page errors. The full Nav responsive/RTL/rail/avatar verifier and Fluent composition/callback verifier also pass. These exclude CSS, React runtime, and SharePoint host costs. This closes the default expansion mismatch; broader Nav visual/accessibility/theme parity and tenant validation remain open. See `scripts/verify-nav-multiple-categories.mjs` and the [benchmark ledger](component-slice-benchmarks.json).

All 160 labels meet 4.5:1 text contrast; the minimum is 11.09:1 on the tenant-teal strong surface. All 80 keyboard focus indicators are at least 2px and meet 3:1; the minimum is 7.89:1. ### Theme and Tag contrast matrix — local Chromium, 2026-11-02

Extended the existing light/dark, five-appearance, four-accent, light/strong-surface, and LTR/RTL matrix across Tag's filled, outline, and brand appearances and neutral, success, warning, danger, and informative colors. All 2,400 Tag text combinations pass the 4.5:1 text threshold; the minimum is 5.06:1 for an outline danger Tag on a strong blue surface. ### Expanded token-pack direction

SharePoint and Fluent tokens remain compatibility inputs, not the ceiling. Use typed semantic roles for repeated product-level decisions, raw Fluent tokens for upstream compatibility, component tokens for one-control tuning, and `customProperties` for app-specific roles. Derive semantic color ramps from the active SharePoint/Fluent accent and surface; expose recipes for brand, platform, and finish looks (including glass) as composed packs. Expand the typed list only when an inventory finds a repeated cross-component need, as with the pressed/subtle accent and focus/disabled border roles; keep pack composition CSS-variable based and preserve explicit precedence. Token inventory found focus, secondary, subtle, and alternate surface roles plus tertiary foreground roles consumed by components but missing from the typed color pack. The typed colors now expose these existing CSS roles, and `verify:theme-role-coverage` prevents new shared component roles from silently bypassing theme definitions. Verify host inheritance, forced colors, strong section backgrounds, RTL, contrast, and component overrides as each role is added.

It measures Link, the visited-link semantic role, Text, and primary Button at rest and hover (300 contrast values); section color scheme follows its declared surface so adaptive iOS colors are measured against the surface they inherit. The prior raw accent link measured 2.47:1 on a strong navy section. Default link color now blends the site accent 65% with active text 35%, while a supplied SharePoint semantic link token takes precedence; typed `colors.link` and `colors.linkVisited` remain explicit overrides. This does not cover every component, palette, browser, state, forced-color mode, or tenant theme; broad theme and accessibility validation remains open. ### Fluent v9 catalog gap checklist

The inventory below is against the 9.75.0 suite package. The [machine-readable family catalog](fluent-v9-catalog-9.75.0.json) records all 51 component and data-rendering families. The current named-API report finds local exported names for 51/51 families; this is a surface-name check only. The catalog still marks every family partial because each needs complete behavior, accessibility, theme, composition, documentation, and performance evidence. InfoButton and InfoLabel were confirmed in source and reclassified from stale `not-implemented` statuses to `partial`. InfoButton supports Fluent's three trigger sizes and typed popover configuration (label, placement, arrow, and focus trapping). Its `inline` prop now applies a low stacking level to its source-order surface, while overlay mode uses the library overlay stacking level; both use the browser top layer to preserve SharePoint overflow escape.

The DatePicker is an additive SharePoint control, not one of those 51 suite families. Fluent's v9 DatePicker remains in its separately named compatibility package; its rename was explicitly chosen because it was not the fully converged component ([Fluent UI issue #27011](https://github.com/microsoft/fluentui/issues/27011)). The work below improves this library's common date-entry gap; it does not count toward core suite parity.

### Parity gap automation

`parity:report`, `parity:props`, and `catalog:check` are the machine-readable discovery commands; `npm run parity:check` now fails on catalog drift or candidate Fluent 9.75 named-API, own-prop, slot, literal-value, or callback-field gaps. The prop extractor resolves local aliases, `Parameters<typeof Component>[0]`, and union/intersection branches; this avoids false missing-prop candidates for overloaded adapters such as `DataGrid`. Safe bulk automation includes refreshing inventory/catalog metadata, validating exact public signatures and exports, applying token/style migrations where the owning engine already supports the behavior, and opening grouped implementation tasks from missing API reports. A missing Fluent prop or component name is only a candidate: do not auto-add a public prop or stub export unless runtime behavior, accessibility, localization, RTL, and custom-element semantics are implemented with it. For each generated patch, rerun the catalog and gap reports, then the family's focused browser verifier and bundle measurement. Reports intentionally leave unresolved type shapes and behavior differences for human review.

#### Contract audit checkpoint — 2026-11-02

The current audit validates 243 catalog entries and finds local names for all 51 Fluent families. Across 231 comparable components it reports no missing own-prop names, slot props, or literal values; 64 callback props have no candidate payload-field gaps. Tree and FlatTree now accept the Fluent `collapseMotion` slot and render nested ARIA groups while keeping flat visible-row navigation; AccordionHeader accepts a `button` slot for button attributes, styles, data selectors, and cancelable click handling. Single non-collapsible Accordion now marks the sole open trigger `aria-disabled` while keeping it focusable. An axe-core fixture covering 20 families exposed an invalid `aria-label` on Tree's generic wrapper; ARIA props now land on the actual `role="tree"` root. It also found a phantom unnamed Combobox fallback trigger, a duplicate ID/invalid cross-shadow label reference for slotted Dropdown buttons, and an invalid `<li role="status">`; fallback controls are removed while a consumer button is assigned and restored when it is removed, slotted buttons receive a direct label that works across the shadow boundary, closed listboxes retain a stable `aria-controls` target, and the no-results message uses a status `div`. SpinButton steppers now measure 24×24px, including at small size; its overall minimum height is 50px. Axe reports no violations, and the fixture manually confirms DatePicker/Combobox `aria-controls` IDs resolve within their shadow roots (axe marks these checks incomplete because it cannot resolve shadow-local references). Focused Tree, Accordion, SpinButton, Combobox, and DatePicker browser verifiers pass. These selected API and runtime checks do not establish full Fluent behavior, accessibility, visual, bundle, or tenant parity; the roadmap remains open.

React 18 emits a custom element's `className` prop as lowercase `classname`, which a Radio runtime check found did not style the component. All 163 statically declared React custom-element roots now normalize `className` into the standard `class` attribute. `npm run parity:check` enforces this mechanically; a focused browser check confirms host and projected label styling. This finding shows why signature audits need behavior checks even when they report zero gaps.

The SharePoint RichTextEditor is an additional domain feature; Fluent v9 does not provide a built-in general-purpose rich-text editor component. It does not close any Fluent catalog gap by itself. It supports sanitized semantic tables, safe links, undo/redo keyboard shortcuts and toolbar actions, RTL-aware toolbar navigation, read-only mode, native form association/required/reset, disabled fieldsets, rich HTML paste, and safe plain-text paste with preserved line boundaries. The local Chromium verifier now checks RTL arrow/Home/End roving focus, the toolbar-to-editor Tab path, and the localized accessible name in addition to existing editing/form/sanitization coverage. Package 1.0.0.79 passed authenticated save/readback on page 058. The tenant sample uses a dedicated FullHtml Note field; the normal restricted multiline-text field silently flattened tables. SharePoint escapes some URL punctuation in storage, so readback compares browser-parsed HTML while still requiring the same content and table structure. Image upload is application-owned through a callback returning a durable HTTP(S) URL; the library does not assume a SharePoint site or asset library. Collaborative editing, assistive-technology review, and current-source tenant validation remain open.

### ButtonGroup — local SPFx-compatible checkpoint

Added `ButtonGroup` and `defineButtonGroup()` as a framework-neutral, tree-shakeable group wrapper. It supplies `role="group"`, supports accessible labels and horizontal/vertical layout, and leaves focus and activation to the actual child buttons (no extra tab stop or custom key model). Local Chromium passed React and custom-element group naming, horizontal and vertical layout, Arabic labeling inside an RTL page, native button Tab order, and zero page errors at 390×844. The library TypeScript build and 109-entry component catalog check pass. Tree-shaken slices: custom element 1,179 raw / 590 gzip / 454 Brotli bytes (3 modules); React adapter 18,998 / 4,527 / 3,877 bytes (5 included modules; React externalized). CSS, React runtime, and SharePoint host are excluded. See `scripts/verify-button-group.mjs` and [slice benchmarks](component-slice-benchmarks.json). This is the basic grouping API; family-wide button states, visual, accessibility, and tenant parity remain open.

### Checkbox tri-state and form participation — local Chromium + SPFx 1.24 build

Checkbox supports Fluent's `false`/`true`/`mixed` state, square/circular shape, medium/large sizes, logical label placement, and `{ checked }` `onChange`; `CheckboxCheckedState` is public and the prior boolean `onCheckedChange` remains compatible. Switch exposes Fluent's `small`/`medium` sizes, `before`/`after`/`above` label positions, and boolean `onChange(event, { checked })`, preserving `onCheckedChange`. Both controls use form-associated custom elements for name/value submission, localized required validity, disabled-fieldset behavior, and form reset. Local Chromium passes React and direct-element paths for mixed semantics, FormData, validity, reset, Switch sizes/labels, RTL label ordering, and zero page errors. SPFx 1.24 solution 1.0.0.138 builds; Heft has zero Jest suites. The latest demo bundle is 504,672 raw / 115,727 gzip / 90,669 Brotli bytes; `.sppkg` is 122,058 bytes. Checkbox leaf slices: element 8,015 / 2,359 / 2,011 bytes and React adapter 26,386 / 6,460 / 5,608; Switch: element 8,098 / 2,379 / 2,029 and React adapter 26,489 / 6,491 / 5,626. One Upgrade and one guarded `AppInstance.Install()` each returned HTTP 200 without transitioning it. No further lifecycle retries should run until that job is inspected.
