### Localized DatePicker calendar — additive SharePoint control

Expanded the former native-only date input to a localized text field and month calendar. The public value remains ISO `YYYY-MM-DD`; `Intl` formats/parses the inherited locale, including localized numeral sets, and selects the locale's first day of week. The calendar supports a seven-column grid, accessible weekday/date names, current/selected/disabled date states, min/max month bounds, pointer selection, type-to-enter, RTL-aware arrow movement, Home/End, PageUp/PageDown and year jumps, Escape focus return, and localized provider-label overrides. Disabled dates remain keyboard reachable but cannot be selected. The form-associated element now exposes value/defaultValue, validity/checkValidity/reportValidity, custom validity, reset/restore, and disabled-fieldset behavior. The existing SPFx example already uses this React control.

Focused Chromium verification passed English and Arabic rendering/parsing, Saturday-first Arabic week, RTL navigation, disabled-date navigation and rejection, month/date bounds, controlled React updates, provider label override, ISO `FormData`, required validity, reset, disabled fieldset, Escape focus return, invalid input, and zero browser errors. The calendar's synchronous open/render cost was 1.5 ms median / 1.9 ms p95 over 20 headless Chromium opens (a DOM microbenchmark, not a user-perceived or SharePoint page metric). Tree-shaken JS slices: native 21,566 raw / 6,298 gzip / 5,554 Brotli bytes; React 40,372 / 10,485 / 9,201 (React externalized; 5 retained modules from 64 graph inputs). SPFx 1.24 build passes at solution 1.0.0.153 / feature 1.0.0.89; bundle `library-demo-web-part_daa6b543b3a115aada19.js` is 584,316 raw / 134,880 gzip / 104,461 Brotli bytes and `.sppkg` is 141,148 bytes. Package artifacts are local; no tenant upload or screen-reader session was run. Further date-format injection, disabled-date callbacks, calendar composition, assistive-technology coverage, and comparison against Fluent's compat component remain open. See `scripts/verify-date-picker.mjs` and the [benchmark ledger](component-slice-benchmarks.json).

### Input slots, event contract, appearances, and native form behavior — local Chromium

Added Fluent-style `contentBefore`/`contentAfter`, `onChange(event, { value })`, the text-input type union, and `filled-darker-shadow`/`filled-lighter-shadow` appearances while retaining `onValueChange`. Input heights now match Fluent at 24/32/40 px; controlled values remain authoritative when the owner does not update them. Native form behavior now submits empty strings and omits values when disabled by a fieldset. Local Chromium verified React and direct-element paths, adornment slots, controlled/default values, form reset, size and shadow appearance styles, FormData, fieldset disabling, and zero page errors. Measured slices: custom element 9,180 raw / 2,828 gzip / 2,442 Brotli bytes (3 modules); React adapter 27,759 / 6,974 / 6,029 bytes (5 included modules, React externalized). A repeated 700×900 Chromium run measured mount p50/p95: 0/0.1 ms (1), 0.3/0.4 ms (20), 1.3/1.8 ms (100). Fifty synchronous input-event samples measured 0/0 ms; these exclude paint and SharePoint host latency. Blue, green, and pink focus tokens on light and dark surfaces measured 3.21:1–5.37:1, exceeding the 3:1 non-text focus-indicator target. Reduced-motion and forced-colors assertions passed. Text contrast still needs site-theme validation. The SPFx 1.24 solution now builds as version 1.0.0.139 (feature 1.0.0.75); its `.sppkg` is 122,710 bytes and demo bundle is 507,390 bytes. It is packaged locally but not uploaded; the tenant catalog remains .138 and the site instance is still Initialized (9). Local Chromium does not substitute for SharePoint theme or assistive-technology validation. See `scripts/verify-input-parity.mjs` and the [benchmark ledger](component-slice-benchmarks.json).

### RadioGroup controlled state, layout, and form behavior — local Chromium

Added Fluent-compatible `defaultValue`, typed `onChange(event, { value })`, and `layout="vertical" | "horizontal" | "horizontal-stacked"`, preserving `value`, `orientation`, and `onValueChange`. `Radio` options can set `labelPosition="after" | "below"` and receive their own `{ value }` change callback. Form association exposes validity methods, uses the browser-localized required message, submits selected empty-string values, omits disabled values, and restores `defaultValue` on reset. Fixed a lifecycle case where a group in an already-disabled fieldset created enabled radio inputs. Local Chromium passed controlled/uncontrolled selection, controlled-value authority when the owner does not update, group and item callbacks, per-item label override, default and reset behavior, FormData, required validity, fieldset disabling, native arrow navigation, RTL order, and zero page errors. One 700×900 Chromium microbenchmark measured group mount p50/p95 at 0.1/0.3 ms (1), 0.6/0.8 ms (20), and 2.5/3.1 ms (100); synchronous selection dispatch was 0/0 ms across 50 samples. These are single-run local samples and exclude paint and SharePoint host latency. Leaf slices: custom element 7,279 raw / 2,256 gzip / 1,907 Brotli bytes (3 modules); React adapter 25,647 / 6,364 / 5,490 bytes (5 included modules, React externalized). CSS, React runtime, and SharePoint host are excluded. This change has not been packaged for the tenant; the installed catalog app is still status 9. See `scripts/verify-radio-group-parity.mjs` and the [benchmark ledger](component-slice-benchmarks.json).

### Radio label and native-control slots — local Chromium

Added React and custom-element `label`, `input`, and `indicator` slot projection. React 18's lowercase `classname` attribute is normalized to the visible label root, so class styling works alongside forwarded inline styles and data attributes. The focused verifier passes custom accessible label content, native input/indicator attributes, controlled and uncontrolled state, form behavior, RTL, dynamic label updates without losing focus, and zero page errors. Current slices: custom element 12,089 raw / 3,578 gzip / 3,117 Brotli bytes; React adapter 36,577 / 9,100 / 7,947 bytes. In one local 700×900 Chromium run, mounting 100 groups measured p50/p95 3.0/3.4 ms; 50 synchronous selection dispatches measured 0/0.1 ms. These are local microbenchmarks, excluding paint, CSS, React runtime, and SharePoint host costs. Tenant package validation and assistive-technology review remain open; see the benchmark ledger.

### RichTextEditor durable image insertion — local browser checkpoint

Added an `onImageUpload(file)` callback to the React adapter and `uploadImage(file)` property to the framework-neutral element. Toolbar image selection and pasted image files use the same upload flow, preserve the editor selection while the asynchronous upload runs, limit files to 10 MiB, and insert the returned durable HTTP(S) URL with a filename-derived alternative text. Sanitization now retains only safe image URLs and bounded width/height attributes; event `spui-image-upload-error` reports callback failures without replacing the current value. Chromium checks pass the upload → inserted HTML value path, reject data URLs and event-handler attributes, and preserve existing table-editing and RTL-label regressions. This is local verification only; tenant persistence still depends on the consuming SharePoint uploader and has not been exercised with the existing stale app instance.

FileUpload is another SharePoint domain addition, not a missing Fluent v9 family. The React adapter and framework-neutral custom element support browse/drop selection, type/count/size checks, removal, localization, required form validation, and `FormData` submission. The consuming web part owns the SharePoint upload request and persistence contract. Current tree-shaken slices are 3,400 B gzip / 2,910 B Brotli for the custom element and 8,909 B / 7,756 B for the React adapter (React externalized). The new `scripts/verify-file-upload.mjs` exposed and fixed two lifecycle defects: reset now clears stale file errors, and `formDisabledCallback` safely handles insertion into a disabled fieldset before the element has rendered. The local browser verifier now passes React/native selection, type/count errors, event payloads, required validity, FormData, reset, disabled-fieldset behavior, and zero page errors. Tenant interaction checks remain unverified until the new web-part package finishes installing on the test site; assistive-technology coverage remains open.

### DataGrid multi-sort implementation checkpoint — local build

Added stable multi-key sorting to the shared DataGrid sorter, React adapter, and custom element. Shift+click adds a secondary key, cycles its direction, and removes it after descending; an ordinary click returns to one sort key. React exposes controlled `multiSort`/`onMultiSortChange` and initial `defaultMultiSort`; the custom element accepts a `sorts` array and includes all keys in `spui-sort-change.detail.sorts` while preserving the original primary `detail.sort`. Sort priority appears in the header and accessible label. Existing `sort`/`defaultSort`/`onSortChange` behavior remains supported.


### DataGrid quick-filter implementation checkpoint — local SPFx build

Added an opt-in labeled search field to the React and native DataGrid paths. It searches all columns by default (individual columns can opt out with `searchable: false`), ignores leading/trailing query whitespace, handles case using the page locale, composes with the existing multi-sort and grouping flows, and uses the library's localized no-results message. React supports controlled `filterValue`/`onFilterValueChange` and uncontrolled `defaultFilterValue`; the custom element exposes `filterable`, `filterValue`, a localized `filter-label`, and bubbling `spui-filter-change` detail.

The SPFx example now shows English and Arabic search labels. Local checks cover case-insensitive matching, whitespace, non-searchable columns, no matches, source-order preservation, and filtering plus sorting. The SPFx production build, catalog check, and catalog parity report pass. The cumulative DataGrid import is 8,801 B gzip / 7,884 B Brotli for the custom element and 10,425 B / 9,299 B for React (4 retained modules, React externalized from 61 graph inputs). These are minified tree-shaken JS only; there is no measured browser keystroke cost yet. Tenant assertions are in the validator but await app installation recovery. Fluent's table primitives do not provide built-in filtering; this is an opt-in SharePoint enhancement. Editable-cell workflows and variable-height rows are implemented; dynamic data sources, batch/clipboard editing, and broad family-level parity remain.

## Delivery phases

### 0. Existing Web Component library pilot — adoption decision pending

The catalog/import research and basic bundle slices are recorded. Fluent UI Web Components and Web Awesome have not been adopted. The parity foundation can continue using platform-native primitives while the same-task SPFx/agent-assembly comparison remains open; no wholesale dependency decision is implied by the small slices.

- Compare the current core with Fluent UI Web Components and Web Awesome on identical SPFx 1.24 imports and page tasks.
- Check iOS base styling + glass overlay, all SharePoint theme tokens, LTR/RTL, form behavior, localization, keyboard/screen-reader behavior, tree shaking, and agent assembly time.
- Keep package versions, license, dependency graph, scripts, compressed bytes, and visual/a11y results with the benchmark.
- Adopt a dependency only when it improves parity or authoring speed enough to justify bundle, styling, API, and upgrade costs. Otherwise keep it as a reference and document the decision.

### 1. Foundations and first control families — in progress

- Define component contracts: attributes/properties, slots, events, controlled/uncontrolled React behavior, focus, disabled/read-only, and accessible naming.
- Keep custom elements as the behavior/rendering source; React wrappers only translate props and custom events.
- Establish semantic SharePoint variables with Fluent token fallbacks; continue forwarding every available SPFx and Fluent token without an allowlist.
- Add opt-in `fluent`, `ios`, `windows`, and `linux` base appearances; let glass derive colors, surfaces, borders, and spacing from any base appearance or tenant theme. The current scale is 1.12 for card/control padding and gaps and 1.04 for control height; custom base color and padding were validated in the tenant. Use adaptive light/dark iOS tokens and honor system contrast settings. The provider now applies its color-scheme to the host. Glass opacity is 88% after the 78% setting missed 4.5:1 on saturated backdrops. Version 1.0.0.15 passed 90 SharePoint/ocean/violet/forest/amber/custom × Fluent/iOS/Windows/Linux/Glass × navy/violet/forest checks at 5.16:1 body text and 4.52:1 action-label minima; this is sample-card coverage, not full-library WCAG certification. Fluent remains the default in SharePoint.
- Add provider and custom-element `lang`/`dir` support, localized built-in strings, RTL-aware controls, and no hard-coded user-facing copy in new components.
- Added composable Nav custom elements and React adapters for native links, category disclosure, nested destinations, dividers, and section headings. Controlled selection/open categories, single/multiple expansion, density, theme styling, RTL-safe logical spacing, and a localized SPFx workspace example are included. Package 1.0.0.74 passed a targeted authenticated check on page 058; complete Nav density/state/keyboard/accessibility parity remains open.
- Nav accepts Avatar and icon slots; disabled native links cancel navigation. At 640px and below it switches to a 44px menu button and a theme-derived logical-start drawer, with localized labels, controlled/default open state, Escape/outside/selection dismissal, focus movement and Escape restoration. Desktop stays a 260px rail by default and can opt into an animated 64px compact rail; no-icon entries get an initial, labels remain screen-reader accessible, and LTR/RTL arrows follow inline direction. Chromium passed React and native compact/expanded state, keyboard and category behavior, 640px responsive drawer, avatar slots and href changes with zero page errors. Latest tree-shaken Nav slices are 24,700 raw / 5,429 gzip / 4,739 Brotli bytes for custom elements and 44,291 / 9,881 / 8,601 bytes for React with React externalized. Compared with the previous Nav measurement, compact rail behavior adds 923 gzip bytes to the native entry and 1,087 bytes to React. Captures: `test-results/nav-compact-native.png`, `test-results/nav-compact-React.png`, `test-results/nav-responsive-native.png`, and `test-results/nav-responsive-React.png`. CSS and SharePoint host costs are excluded; family-wide density/state/contrast review and authenticated tenant validation remain open.
- Cover form controls (Button, Field/Input, TextArea, Checkbox, Radio, Switch, Select, Combobox, SpinButton, Slider, DatePicker) and feedback/status (Badge, Spinner, ProgressBar, MessageBar, Toast).
- Added `Text` with semantic tags, sizes 100–1200, weights, base/monospace/numeric fonts, logical alignment, emphasis, wrapping, and truncation. Added React `Label` plus a framework-neutral native-label factory so `for` association remains browser-native. Local desktop/mobile browser checks passed semantic heading/emphasis, theme font tokens, RTL alignment, truncation, in-place size updates, required-marker accessibility, label activation, and overflow checks. esbuild 0.28.2 slices: Text 1,552 B gzip / 1,292 B Brotli custom element and 4,799 B / 4,130 B React adapter; Label 549 B / 442 B native factory and 3,610 B / 3,141 B React adapter, with React externalized. These are minified tree-shaken JS slices, not a SharePoint runtime comparison; family-level Fluent API/a11y/theme coverage remains partial.
- Tenant-verified Checkbox, Switch, TextArea, Select, RadioGroup, Spinner, MessageBar, Combobox selection/Escape, Slider keyboard/bounds, and DatePicker ISO value/bounds. SpinButton ArrowUp, numeric update, and min/max/step constraints passed. RadioGroup native arrow navigation, required validity, FormData submission, and empty-state form reset passed.

### 2. Overlays and navigation — in progress

- Dialog is implemented as a framework-neutral custom element backed by native `<dialog>`, with controlled React props, localized close label, title/actions slots, modal and non-modal modes, and dismissible behavior. Tenant checks passed for focus return, Escape, close action, non-dismissible behavior, and 8 open/close cycles. Its isolated leaf import is 1,833 B gzip / 1,571 B Brotli across 3 modules.
- ProgressBar exposes Fluent `color` (`brand|success|warning|error`), `shape`, and `thickness`, with `max=1`, accessible value bounds, theme-derived colors, RTL-aware indeterminate motion, reduced-motion support, and forced-colors handling. Local Chromium validates the React and custom-element paths, token inheritance, updates, clamping, and motion settings. Its current isolated import size is recorded in the benchmark ledger; prior tenant evidence applies only to the earlier implementation.
- Implemented controls still need validation-state composition, disabled fieldset/reset coverage across every form-associated element, and broad behavior parity.
- Implemented in this slice: single/multiple disclosure Accordion, assertive/polite themed Alert and reduced-motion/forced-color aware Skeleton. Accordion slice sizes are 3,918 B raw / 1,543 B gzip / 1,274 B Brotli for the custom element, and 19,610 B / 4,869 B / 4,182 B for the React adapter import (React external). Breadcrumb provides labelled navigation, ordered item semantics, logical separators, tab and arrow focus modes, Home/End, and RTL-aware movement among links. Package 1.0.0.61 passed authenticated tenant checks; its leaf is 1,966 B gzip / 1,683 B Brotli for custom elements and 5,913 B / 5,148 B for React with React externalized. Tabs provides controlled/default selection, roving focus, optional select-on-focus, Enter/Space activation, disabled skipping, orientation, four appearances, panel associations, and RTL-aware arrows through React and custom elements. SPFx 1.24 package 1.0.0.65 passed the full tenant suite on page 050, including all four appearance surfaces and hover states. Its leaf is 3,254 B gzip / 2,835 B Brotli for custom elements and 6,635 B / 5,819 B for React with React externalized. Tooltip has a custom-element core and React adapter with focus/hover opening, Escape dismissal, `aria-describedby`, delay, controlled open state, and logical placement. Its collision handling now flips on the main axis, clamps to viewport margins, and repositions on resize/scroll and observed size changes. Touch pointer entry does not trigger hover; focus still does. A single visible tooltip is enforced, Escape is handled at document capture, and backgrounding dismisses it. Local Chromium passes top/bottom and LTR/RTL side collisions, resize repositioning, one-visible coordination, Escape/background dismissal, and touch hover suppression. Tooltip slices after the change: custom element 2,323 B gzip / 2,006 B Brotli; React 6,360 B / 5,528 B (React externalized). See `scripts/verify-tooltip-positioning.mjs` and the [benchmark ledger](component-slice-benchmarks.json). Previous package 1.0.0.66 tenant evidence covers focus, hover, Escape and description association, not these current changes. Popover provides a top-layer, non-modal surface with a trigger slot/React `trigger` prop, label/title association, controlled open state, outside/Escape dismissal, focus return, theme styling, and logical placements. Package 1.0.0.71 passed Arabic (`lang=ar`, `dir=rtl`) logical placement checks. Menu now has all named Fluent family parts in React and framework-neutral elements. It supports controlled/default checked values, checkbox/radio/switch/link actions, icon/subtext/shortcut slots, persistent selection, default/controlled open state, hover/context opening, close-on-scroll, inline rendering, placement/alignment/offset, disabled focus targets, nested menus, typeahead, and RTL-aware focus restoration. Local Chromium gates pass composition, selection, persistence, hover, scroll, positioning, LTR/RTL submenus and context-menu invocation. Package 1.0.0.72 is historical tenant evidence only; current source still needs authenticated tenant, assistive-technology, family contrast, and bundle measurement gates. Drawer uses a native modal dialog surface with logical side or top/bottom placement, four sizes, dismissible controls, localized close labels, and title/actions slots; package 1.0.0.73 passed the full tenant validator, including eight open cycles, Escape/backdrop closure, an Arabic close label, and RTL logical-end placement on page 058. Its leaf is 1,983 B Brotli for custom elements and 4,969 B for React with React externalized. Drawer supports dismissible non-modal outside-press/Escape dismissal and persistent mode; broader responsive/focus/scroll coverage remains open. Menu bar/context-menu patterns, collision and nested-overlay coordination, TeachingPopover, and Nav also remain in this historical roadmap slice. Tabs, Tooltip, Popover, Menu, Drawer, and Toolbar still need family-wide parity and visual/state coverage.
- Toast now has framework-neutral Toast/Toaster elements and React `Toast`, `Toaster`, and `useToastController` APIs. In addition to dispatch/update/dismiss, the controller exposes pause/play by ID; visible Toasts support ArrowUp/ArrowDown movement, Delete/Escape dismissal, focus restoration, timers pause while the region is focused, and timeout `0`/`-1` means persistent. A bounded visible limit, stable priority queue, and local enqueue measurements are validated; screen-reader and tenant validation plus visual state/contrast breadth remain open.
- SearchBox now has a native `type="search"` core and React adapter, controlled/uncontrolled value support, a clear action, native form value/reset/required validation, Enter-to-submit, theme tokens, named CSS parts, localized clear text, RTL-safe logical layout, and Fluent `contentBefore`/`contentAfter`/`dismiss` slots in React plus kebab-case native slots. Chromium verifies slot order, native slot composition, action isolation, and mouse/keyboard clear behavior. Full visual/forced-color, assistive-technology, and authenticated SPFx parity remain open.
- ColorPicker now has a native color input, interactive HSV hue and saturation/brightness controls, controlled/default HSV object state (`h/s/v/a`) in React and custom elements, HEX/RGB editing, theme-derived SharePoint accent/status swatches, custom swatches, opacity, native form value/reset/validation, localized labels/errors, and composable React/custom-element area, hue, saturation/value, and alpha controls. `ColorArea`, `ColorSlider`, and `AlphaSlider` also work independently with controlled/default HSV objects and per-control change callbacks. Authenticated SPFx validation, additional color formats, screen-reader review, and family-level accessibility/performance parity remain open.
- Toolbar now has framework-neutral and React APIs for labelled groups and orientation-aware separators. One roving focus sequence spans every group and native/open-shadow control, skips disabled commands, honors RTL and configurable wrapping, and observes late-rendered shadow controls before the next paint. Added ToolbarButton/ToolbarToggleButton aliases, ToolbarRadioGroup/ToolbarRadioButton, named controlled/default checked values, and ToolbarDivider. Focused Chromium checks pass controlled React selection, native default/selection, radiogroup semantics, disabled skipping, and aliases; the SPFx 1.24 production build passes. Tree-shaken Toolbar element/React slices measure 3,832/8,070 gzip bytes; radio-button slices measure 1,652/5,678 gzip bytes. Tenant validation and broader visual/accessibility/performance comparisons remain open.
- Persona now composes the existing Avatar with primary/secondary/tertiary identity text, presence indicators and localized labels, size/layout variants, and avatar/details slots through React and custom-element APIs. The people family is partial; broader Fluent identity variants, active/inactive semantics, accessible interactive patterns, and tenant validation remain open.
- Specify focus entry/return, Escape behavior, dismissal, portal/stack behavior, arrow-key patterns, and SharePoint canvas clipping before implementation.

### 3. Collections and data display

- Implemented: Avatar, Persona, AvatarGroup, Card, Image, Divider, Accordion, Tree, Tag/TagGroup/TagPicker, slot-composed SharePoint ViewCard, fixed or opt-in measured-height VirtualList/Virtualizer, manual localized/RTL Carousel, and DataGrid with stable item identity, sorting, selection, keyboard cell movement, bounded row rendering, pointer/keyboard resizing with min/max bounds, double-click auto-fit, and controlled pointer/keyboard column reordering with RTL key behavior. Tree supports nested data, accessible hierarchy metadata, controlled/default expansion and selection, roving focus, locale-aware typeahead, and RTL-aware horizontal keys. Tag supports theme-derived appearances, semantic colors, size/shape variants, and accessible dismiss controls. TagPicker provides searchable multi-select, keyboard selection/removal, disabled and grouped options, controlled values, and native form association; its existing SPFx 1.24 tenant checks passed. Rating provides native radio semantics, whole/half-step scoring, controlled/default values, keyboard selection, localization, and custom-form support; its SPFx 1.24 tenant run passed controlled half-step changes, arrow navigation, RTL, and Arabic item labels. Its local verifier now also covers React/native FormData, required validity, reset, and disabled-fieldset behavior. Rating leaf size: 2,398 B gzip / 2,064 B Brotli for custom elements and 5,794 B / 5,024 B for the React adapter with React externalized. Remaining: broader DataGrid family accessibility/state comparison and richer List semantics.
- AvatarGroup stacks `Avatar` and `AvatarGroupItem` entries, inherits group size, limits the visible count, and can show named overflow entries in `AvatarGroupPopover`. The popover has a localized count or icon trigger, controlled-open callback, theme-aware list surface, Escape and outside dismissal, and logical placement. React adapters and framework-neutral `spui-avatar-group-item` / `spui-avatar-group-popover` elements are catalogued. SPFx 1.24 package 1.0.0.93 served its expected hashed bundle on page 058; tenant checks passed for the LTR/RTL triggers, accessible named member list, and Escape dismissal. Entrance motion is 140 ms and becomes 0 ms under reduced motion. Measured import slices (raw/gzip/Brotli): AvatarGroup custom element 15,021/4,374/3,796 B and React 31,885/7,869/6,853 B; AvatarGroupItem custom element 12,902/3,941/3,447 B and React 29,927/7,431/6,488 B; AvatarGroupPopover custom element 12,280/3,901/3,389 B and React 29,095/7,436/6,486 B. React is external; estimates exclude React runtime, CSS, and SharePoint host. Heft found no Jest suites, so these behavior checks are the authenticated Playwright tenant run, not a full test-suite claim.
- Card now exports `Card`, `CardHeader`, `CardPreview`, and `CardFooter` through React and independent custom-element definitions. The base supports filled, filled-alternative, outline, and subtle surfaces, small/medium/large spacing, and horizontal/vertical composition. Header has image/title/description/action slots; Preview supports media/custom content and a theme-aware logo overlay with logical edge bleed; Footer wraps actions and aligns its secondary action to the logical end. Package 1.0.0.95 passed the original composition checks on the published SPFx 1.24 page. Card also supports controlled/default selection through a native checkbox, disabled selection, localized label override, keyboard activation, RTL placement, and interaction isolation for nested links/buttons. Local Chromium passed these checks at 390×844 with no page errors. React and custom-element Card support Fluent-style `off`, `no-tab`, `tab-exit`, and `tab-only` focus modes; Chromium passed Enter/Escape, forward/backward trapping, tab-exit, tab-only, nested shadow controls, and interactive-handler defaults. The focusout boundary check descends through nested shadow roots. A 50-sample synthetic selection `change` dispatch measured p50 0 ms / p95 0.1 ms; it excludes layout and paint and is not a user-perceived latency claim. Tenant validation for the current selection and focus-mode code remains blocked by the status-9 installed instance and stale `.134` asset; local results do not establish tenant behavior. Refresh Card bundle measurements after recording this slice in the benchmark ledger.
- The same tenant run passed InfoButton open/focus, Escape close and outside dismissal, and InfoLabel native label association plus keyboard focus order. The validator waits for the dialog surface's focus setup before sampling; its previous immediate sample raced the component's animation frame. This is a harness synchronization correction, not a component behavior change.
- DataGrid now supports double-click auto-fit on the existing resize handle in both the React adapter and custom element. It measures only currently rendered rows plus the header, so the work stays bounded under virtualization, then clamps to the column's configured min/max. On SPFx package 1.0.0.84, the React grid shrank the Task column from 448 px to its 220 px minimum. The 5,000-row grid mounted 11 initial rows and 12 at the end; the jump to its final row took 84 ms. Pointer resize, keyboard resize, sorting, selection, and Arabic RTL resize labeling passed. DataGrid slice: 5,700 B gzip / 5,049 B Brotli custom element and 7,571 B / 6,739 B React adapter, with React externalized.
- Added standalone `Input` through the React and custom-element APIs. It supports common native input attributes/types, controlled and default values, size/appearance, prefix/suffix slots, custom-element form association, native validation/reset, and disabled fieldsets. The agent guidance calls out that a document-level label cannot name an input hidden behind the shadow boundary; provide `aria-label`/`aria-labelledby` or use `TextField` for the library-rendered associated label. Package 1.0.0.87 passed the live SPFx React example, accessible role/name and consumer `data-testid` selectors, input keyboard order, value update, required validity, FormData submission, default-value reset, and reduced-motion checks. Input leaf size: 2,715 B gzip / 2,342 B Brotli for the custom element and 6,212 B / 5,400 B for the React adapter, React externalized. The Fluent named-API report is now missing 10/51 families; this counts API names, not full parity.
- Button now covers `CompoundButton`, `ToggleButton`, and `SplitButton` in both the React adapter and framework-neutral custom-element API. CompoundButton exposes title/icon/description slots and accessible description; ToggleButton uses native `aria-pressed` with controlled/uncontrolled state; SplitButton composes the existing Button and Menu engines, and marks the inner segment corners so the two controls read as one split action. Button additionally supports five appearances, three sizes, three shapes, and icon-only sizing. SPFx package 1.0.0.90 passed tenant checks for accessible CompoundButton naming/description, controlled ToggleButton press/unpress, SplitButton primary and menu actions, and Tab focus across each button. The unchanged SharePoint page loaded exact bundle `library-demo-web-part_dfeb0a827106f8d04cd2.js`; no errors originated in the component. The one certificate error observed in a separate diagnostic came from SharePoint's `config.fp.measure.office.com` telemetry endpoint. Final slice sizes (custom element gzip/Brotli; React adapter gzip/Brotli, React externalized): CompoundButton 1,931/1,636 B and 5,301/4,551 B; ToggleButton 1,701/1,407 B and 5,175/4,423 B; SplitButton 891/693 B and 8,531/7,499 B. These are tree-shaken JavaScript estimates excluding React runtime, CSS, and SharePoint host. The live demo's web-part asset transferred 101,399 bytes and decoded to 317,203 bytes; its per-page interaction samples remain host-inclusive. This closes the Button family's named API gaps, not button-group parity. The focused tenant capture is `test-results/library-demo/button-family.png`.
- UX audit on the published package 1.0.0.87 now checks card heading/muted-copy contrast in Fluent, iOS, Windows, and Linux presets: 12.98:1 and 6.46:1 respectively. Dark iOS card text measured 9.77:1. A 1440×960 desktop page and a separately reloaded 390×844 mobile page both had zero page-level horizontal overflow; the grid's internal horizontal scroller is intentional. Motion checks observed Input transitions at 120 ms and Spinner rotation at 750 ms, then confirmed both stop under `prefers-reduced-motion`; skeleton motion also stops. One live visit measured 16.7 ms library mount, 49.5/68.7 ms Save click p50/p95, 54.5/61.7 ms Dialog open p50/p95, and 29.9 ms Input update. The web-part resource was 98,438 transfer bytes / 98,138 encoded bytes / 306,474 decoded bytes. These are single-page observations, include React and SharePoint host cost, and do not establish a speed advantage over Fluent. No page or console errors occurred. The screenshots and detailed JSON are in `test-results/library-demo/`; the selector and contrast checks are in `scripts/tenant-validate.mjs`.
- DataGrid tenant run on SPFx package 1.0.0.50 rendered 5,000 rows with 11 initial / 12 end rows mounted; sorting, selection, pointer resize, keyboard resize, bounds, RTL behavior and Arabic accessible-name updates passed. Its React slice is 7,203 B gzip / 6,423 B Brotli with React externalized; the custom-element leaf is 5,539 B / 4,908 B. It uses opaque base theme surfaces under glass so content behind the table does not show through. This is one run, not a percentile benchmark.
- Tree tenant validation on SPFx package 1.0.0.51 passed three-level hierarchy metadata, keyboard focus and expand/collapse, typeahead, selection, Arabic labels, and RTL key reversal. Its custom-element slice is 3,086 B gzip / 2,718 B Brotli; its React adapter is 5,575 B / 4,905 B with React externalized. The tree renders six initially visible nodes; this is a small navigation fixture, not a large-tree virtualization benchmark.
- Tag/TagGroup tenant validation on package 1.0.0.55 passed pointer and keyboard dismissal, disabled-state behavior, provider-driven Arabic accessible names, and RTL group direction. The two-component custom-element slice is 1,723 B gzip / 1,417 B Brotli; the React adapter slice is 5,048 B / 4,329 B with React externalized. TagPicker is implemented with framework-neutral and React APIs. Package 1.0.0.56 passed its tenant behavior checks and visible fill/border checks in Fluent, iOS, Windows, Linux, and Glass. Its custom-element slice is 3,823 B gzip / 3,314 B Brotli; the React slice is 7,122 B / 6,238 B with React externalized.
- Build virtualization only with evidence from representative SharePoint list sizes; keep data and rendering adapters replaceable.
- Add dynamic data adapters and slot-composed view cards (document, person, task, approval, and list-item summaries) from the same primitives. Keep data loading, selection, actions, fields, and rendering overrideable.
- Ship realistic SPFx examples backed by typed sample records, including loading/empty/error states, property controls, responsive views, and custom actions.

### 4. Theme, platform, and internationalization completeness

- Light/dark/custom themes, high contrast/forced colors, section backgrounds, tenant palette changes, RTL, localization, reduced motion, zoom/reflow, and screen-reader behavior.
- Document SPFx theme provider setup and supported React versions; keep non-React examples first-class.

### 5. Agent and release experience

- Add a machine-readable component manifest, stable import examples, prop/event/slot tables, recipes, usage constraints, and concise accessibility guidance.
- Add agent-ready task recipes for common SharePoint cards/forms, direct custom-element and React examples, and customization of slots, tokens, fonts, and actions.
- Build a GitHub-hosted docs and presentation site with searchable coverage of every component, plain-language purpose/constraints, API and styling references, working examples, theme/RTL demos, and evidence-backed performance and accessibility notes. Source its inventory from `components.json` so new APIs appear without a second hand-maintained index.
- Provide copyable SPFx CLI examples, deterministic scaffolding, troubleshooting, and upgrade notes.
- Package with tree-shakeable entry points and no mandatory React runtime for custom-element consumers.
- Let SPFx packages select components/views through leaf entrypoints; verify production bundles exclude unselected families and their optional dependencies.
- Run `npm run parity:report` to compare Fluent's named API examples against local public React exports, and `npm run catalog:check --workspace @vilius/sharepoint-ui` to catch inventory/signature drift. These are fast surface checks; family behavior, accessibility, theme, performance, docs, and tenant proof still require their own gates.
- Run `npm run measure:component -- <ReactComponentName>` for repeatable minified tree-shaken custom-element and React slice sizes (raw/gzip/Brotli and module count). This is a development estimate; tenant bundle transfer, CSS, React runtime, and host cost stay separate.
- Publish the first reviewed npm release deliberately, then enable CI release automation with provenance, version/tag checks, package dry-run, SBOM/license review, and install-from-tarball validation. Keep secrets and publish permissions out of pull-request jobs.

## Differentiators to build toward

- **SharePoint-aware views:** document, person, task, approval, and list-item cards with stable fields for common SharePoint data, while keeping slots/renderers available for tenant-specific fields.
- **Compose-first cards:** a predictable base structure with named slots, CSS parts, and typed fields. Keep tenant-specific content and actions replaceable without forking the card.
- **Data rendering that stays fast:** windowed lists and grids, incremental pages, stable item keys, empty/loading/error states, and keyboard/screen-reader metadata. Virtualize only large collections; keep ordinary lists simple.
- **Pick-your-bundle packaging:** leaf entrypoints and tree-shakeable React exports so a web part includes only the controls, view cards, and optional adapters it uses.
- **Agent-readable contracts:** a small machine-readable inventory of props, events, slots, accessibility requirements, bundle cost, recipes, and supported SPFx/runtime versions.
- **Style without forking:** CSS variables, `::part`, custom fonts, appearance presets, full host palette/theme pass-through, and an optional expanded design-token pack. Keep SharePoint/Fluent inheritance as the default; let teams opt into a named look, provide a brand color for derived palettes, or override semantic/component tokens locally. Stone remains the no-host-theme accent fallback.
- **Locale by construction:** caller-supplied strings, `lang`/`dir`, bidi-safe layout, and `Intl` based date/number formatting so views do not embed English-only assumptions.

## High-value additions backlog — implement after the current parity pass

These additions improve common SharePoint work beyond the baseline catalog. They are deliberately tracked separately from parity so they do not hide missing baseline APIs.

| Component | Candidate addition | Why it helps | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| AvatarGroup | Open a labelled overflow popover with named members and a count or icon trigger | Lets people identify overflow members without expanding a dense row | Implemented and tenant-validated on SPFx 1.24 package 1.0.0.93; measured as separate import slices |
| Card | Compose file identity, edge-to-edge preview, logo, and file actions from named slots | Gives agents a reusable SharePoint file view they can extend without cloning layout | Implemented; tenant validation and measurement pending |
| DataGrid | Double-click a column's existing resize handle to auto-fit that column to currently rendered content | Makes common SharePoint grids quicker to tune without adding a second control; bounded work stays aligned with virtualization | Implemented and tenant-validated in React adapter; custom-element runtime still needs direct tenant coverage |
| DataGrid | Persist selection scope across provider query changes and support provider-backed bulk clipboard transactions | Keeps select-all-matching stable across query updates and enables large-range copy/paste without loading the entire dataset | Implemented in React and native indexed sources; browser verified, SPFx 1.24 build passed |
| DataGrid | Group rows by a selected column with accessible expand/collapse, counts, and controllable group state | Makes large SharePoint task and document grids easier to scan without forcing every consumer to build grouping logic | Implemented and tenant-validated in React and custom-element APIs on SPFx 1.24 package 1.0.0.122; group display is part of bounded virtualization |
| Toolbar | Add command groups and separators while reusing the library's existing button styling | Organizes longer command strips and stays visually consistent with other controls | Implemented in React and native elements; browser verified |
| Nav | Support avatar/picture and icon slots, plus an explicit full-width expanded rail mode | Makes agent-built SharePoint app navigation look like a real workspace and remain discoverable | Avatar slot and responsive mobile drawer implemented; full-width expanded mode queued |
| Drawer | Add persistent non-modal behavior with explicit dismissal policy | Keeps supporting panes available without blocking the page while preserving predictable focus and dismissal | Implemented and locally verified |
| Carousel | Add an optional thumbnail-preview strip for image-focused galleries, with roving keyboard focus and localized accessible labels | Lets users navigate media collections by visual preview without making ordinary SharePoint records heavier | Queued |
| Overflow | Add persisted compact-mode preference for responsive command shelves | Lets a SharePoint workspace retain a user's density preference across visits | Queued |
| SwatchPicker | Add multi-select and saved tenant palettes | Lets design tools offer reusable brand palettes and allow several colors where the workflow needs them | Queued |

## Gates for each component family

1. API/behavior contract and React + custom-element examples reviewed.
2. Keyboard, semantic role/name, focus, disabled, error, and state coverage; include automated accessibility checks where the repo test workflow supports them.
   - Forms also need native submission, reset, required/validity, and disabled-fieldset behavior before claiming form-control parity.
3. Verify light/dark/high-contrast and responsive behavior, then RTL and reduced motion where relevant.
4. Record minified, gzip, Brotli, and dependency/module counts for a realistic import slice; compare against Fluent 9.75.0 with CSS and equivalent behavior caveats stated.
5. Measure cold/warm mount and primary interactions at p50/p95, both in isolation and on the authenticated SPFx 1.24 page. Include 1/20/100-instance checks for repeated controls.
6. Keep host-inclusive page costs separate from component-local timings; capture rendered desktop/mobile states and preserve raw evidence.

## Initial budgets and evidence policy

- Keep the shared runtime small; publish per-family and per-entrypoint gzip/Brotli sizes so applications only pay for imported components. Each family must justify growth with measured behavior and parity.
- Set per-component budgets after the first equivalent benchmark; do not claim speed from an unstyled or behaviorally smaller comparison.
- Publish cold/warm results, sample count, browser/host, package version, and confidence limits. Treat single-visit tenant figures as observations, not stable percentiles.
- A parity row is complete only when its behavior, theme, accessibility, docs, and measurement gates pass; an API stub does not count.
- iOS, Windows, and Linux are opt-in, token-based appearances, not claims of pixel-perfect OS replicas. Glass is a derived finish: it uses the selected base/tenant colors, then calculates translucency, border, hover, status, blur, and spacing values from them.

### Field composition and validation checkpoint — SPFx package 1.0.0.99

`Field` now composes one native or custom control with a visible label, hint, required state, validation message, and theme-derived error/warning/success state. It is available as a React adapter and as the framework-neutral `createFormField(control, options)` factory. On the authenticated Vilius tenant, all Field checks passed: accessible name and hint association, native required state, error message/`aria-invalid`, clearing the error after a valid value, and an unfocused error border that resolves to the SharePoint error text color (`rgb(164, 38, 44)`). The validator now waits for the 120 ms border transition before sampling and targets the validation-message region rather than the decorative `!` text.

The page served exact expected bundle `library-demo-web-part_22ec054fbcbf3ab74cba.js`. This single cache-disabled visit measured 337,495 decoded / 108,780 encoded / 109,080 transferred bytes for the demo bundle, 354.7 ms resource duration, 476 ms FCP, 699.4 ms DOM interactive, zero navigation long tasks, and 139 MB host-inclusive JS heap. Field factory slice: 4,600 raw / 1,644 gzip / 1,424 Brotli bytes across 3 modules; React adapter: 20,233 / 4,903 / 4,291 bytes across 5 retained modules, with React externalized. These are tree-shaken estimates; page timings are one host-inclusive visit, not a speed comparison. The whole authenticated validator passed with zero page, console, or resource errors. See [Field screenshot](../test-results/library-demo/field-validation.png), [full metrics](../test-results/library-demo/metrics.json), and [slice benchmarks](component-slice-benchmarks.json).

SharePoint App Catalog reports 1.0.0.99 deployed, enabled, and valid, while `InstalledVersion` remains 1.0.0.35. The guarded Upgrade call returned HTTP 200 but did not change that value after 20 seconds. Runtime evidence independently confirms the 1.0.0.99 asset is served by the published page. Treat installation metadata divergence as unresolved; do not infer that InstalledVersion is repaired from the successful runtime check. Current named-API gap report: 5/51 Fluent v9 families still have at least one missing named API (Carousel, Overflow, SwatchPicker, TeachingPopover, Virtualizer); this report does not establish behavioral parity for the other families.

### Image composition and tenant checkpoint — SPFx package 1.0.0.100

Image is a native `<img>` React adapter and `createImage` factory; it adds no registered custom element. The API forwards browser-native `srcSet`, `sizes`, intrinsic dimensions, `loading`, `decoding`, and other image attributes, while adding `fit`, `shape`, block display, theme-derived border, and tokenized shadow. The Card example now uses this Image API for its actual document preview.

The authenticated page passed alt-text, loaded intrinsic dimensions (620×350), lazy/async loading, cover fit, border, shadow, Card accessibility, and edge-to-edge preview checks. It served expected bundle `library-demo-web-part_e68af39d5eeeae6a8236.js`. This single cache-disabled visit measured 338,729 decoded / 109,407 encoded / 109,707 transferred bytes, 321.1 ms bundle-resource duration, 468 ms FCP, zero navigation long tasks, and 103 MB host-inclusive heap. Image factory slice: 880 raw / 491 gzip / 403 Brotli bytes across 2 modules; React adapter: 16,729 / 3,697 / 3,185 bytes across 4 retained modules, React externalized. The whole authenticated suite passed without page, console, or resource errors. Figures are single-visit, host-inclusive, and not a speed comparison. See [Card Image screenshot](../test-results/library-demo/card-composition.png), [full metrics](../test-results/library-demo/metrics.json), and [slice benchmarks](component-slice-benchmarks.json).

App Catalog reports 1.0.0.100 deployed, enabled, and valid; `InstalledVersion` remains 1.0.0.35 despite Upgrade returning HTTP 200. Runtime verification confirms the current .100 bundle is served. Installation metadata divergence remains unresolved. The named API report now finds 5/51 families missing names: Carousel, Overflow, SwatchPicker, TeachingPopover, and Virtualizer.

### Carousel implementation checkpoint — SPFx package 1.0.0.104

Added `Carousel` as a native scroll-snap custom element with a thin React adapter and a reusable `ViewCard` example. It provides manual previous/next/slide navigation, optional wrapping, controlled/default index state, change events, keyboard arrow/Home/End navigation, RTL direction, localized labels, reduced-motion behavior, forced-colors focus, touch scrolling, and generated slide labels that preserve consumer-authored accessible names. Programmatic slide selection stays stable during smooth motion and reconciles at `scrollend`, avoiding stale intermediate-index events. The tenant harness now waits for scroll completion instead of assuming a fixed short animation time. Autoplay is omitted so SharePoint content does not move without an explicit user action. Tenant checks cover accessible region naming, slide count/labels, previous/next controls, keyboard navigation, wrapping, and a screenshot.

Final tree-shaken slice: custom element 11,450 raw / 3,501 gzip / 3,039 Brotli bytes; React adapter 28,708 / 7,157 / 6,266 bytes, React externalized (5 retained modules from 57 graph inputs). Live SPFx 1.24 validation passed role/name, slide count, authored/generated labels, next/previous, settled keyboard movement, wrap, RTL keyboard, and Arabic localized controls/role description. Each slide measured 1,117 px in a 1,117 px viewport. The cache-disabled authenticated visit served `library-demo-web-part_8763064bde057ae57c7c.js` (352,306 decoded / 113,813 encoded / 114,113 transferred bytes); FCP 668 ms, DOM interactive 670.2 ms, load 1,562.8 ms, zero navigation long tasks, three app interaction long tasks, 103 MB host-inclusive heap, and 18.5 ms library mount. The page exposed 180 SP theme entries and 459 Fluent v9 tokens. This is one host-inclusive visit, not a speed comparison. See the [Carousel screenshot](../test-results/library-demo/carousel.png), [full tenant metrics](../test-results/library-demo/metrics.json), and [slice benchmarks](component-slice-benchmarks.json). Site-collection catalog reports .104 valid/deployed/enabled; InstalledVersion remains .35. The parity report now finds missing named APIs in 4/51 families: Overflow, SwatchPicker, TeachingPopover, and Virtualizer.

### Overflow implementation checkpoint — SPFx package 1.0.0.106

Added `Overflow`, `OverflowItem`, and `OverflowDivider` as a framework-neutral custom-element engine with a React adapter backed by the existing Menu. It measures rendered widths on resize and content/font changes, hides lower-priority actions first, honors pinned and minimum-visible items, hides empty group dividers, and exposes visible/overflow IDs through an event and data attributes. It keeps controls in place and renders separate menu representations so focusable actions are not cloned or reparented. The host exposes the labelled `group` role; the first tenant pass caught and fixed an earlier implementation that put the role only on an internal shadow element. The tenant validator now uses the menu accessibility snapshot for shadow-scoped item counts and page-level role locators for activation.

SPFx 1.24 tenant validation passed responsive priority selection, pinned Save, group divider behavior, named group semantics, trigger show/hide at 320/800 px, overflow menu content, Archive selection, and the full existing page checks. The page served the expected `library-demo-web-part_fa839cb37ae8b83590bf.js` (362,855 decoded / 117,382 encoded / 117,682 transferred bytes). This cache-disabled visit measured FCP 604 ms, DOM interactive 604.8 ms, load 1,298.6 ms, zero navigation long tasks, 109 MB host-inclusive JS heap, and 21.7 ms library mount. The separate app-wide interaction metrics include SharePoint work; this is one host-inclusive observation, not a performance comparison. The page exposed 180 SharePoint theme entries and 459 Fluent v9 tokens. Zero page, console, resource, or missing profile-photo errors were observed. See the [Overflow screenshot](../test-results/library-demo/overflow.png), [full tenant metrics](../test-results/library-demo/metrics.json), and [slice benchmarks](component-slice-benchmarks.json).

Tree-shaken slice sizes: custom-element 7,336 raw / 2,445 gzip / 2,134 Brotli bytes (3 retained modules); React adapter 41,766 / 10,286 / 9,105 bytes (7 retained modules, React externalized). These exclude React runtime, CSS assets, and SharePoint host costs. Site-collection catalog reports .106 deployed, enabled, and valid while `InstalledVersion` remains stale at .35; the page served the exact .106 bundle, so runtime package identity is confirmed but SharePoint's installation metadata divergence remains unresolved. The named-API gap report now finds 3/51 Fluent families with missing names: SwatchPicker, TeachingPopover, and Virtualizer. Behavioral parity remains open across the wider catalog.

Added `SwatchPicker`, `ColorSwatch`, `ImageSwatch`, `EmptySwatch`, and `SwatchPickerRow` as native custom elements with React adapters. The SharePoint example uses the existing document preview asset for an image swatch and includes a managed/disabled color. Multi-select and saved palettes remain separate high-value follow-ups.

It exposed 180 SharePoint theme entries and 459 Fluent v9 tokens, with zero page, console, resource, or profile-photo errors. See the [SwatchPicker screenshot](../test-results/library-demo/swatch-picker.png), [full tenant metrics](../test-results/library-demo/metrics.json), and [slice benchmarks](component-slice-benchmarks.json).

The site-collection catalog reports .107 deployed, enabled, and valid while `InstalledVersion` stays stale at .35; the exact .107 bundle is served at runtime.
