### TeachingPopover implementation checkpoint — SPFx package 1.0.0.112

Added the complete named React family: `TeachingPopover`, trigger/surface/header/title/body/footer parts, and carousel/card/footer/button/nav/page-count parts. The root composes through the existing native Popover positioning engine, with an arrow, opt-in focus trap, Escape dismissal, and focus return. Carousel state can be controlled or uncontrolled; next/previous and direct step selection announce the current count. The authenticated SPFx example is a localized English/Arabic two-step workspace tour.

The Vilius tenant on page 058 served the expected `library-demo-web-part_77ebc0d89d0fee8f891d.js`. Targeted live checks passed initial focus, Shift+Tab and Tab wrapping, step navigation and page count, finish and Escape dismissal, and trigger focus restoration, with no page errors. The full authenticated regression validator also passed with zero page, console, resource, or profile-photo errors. The cache-disabled page observation measured 385,168 decoded / 124,144 encoded / 124,444 transferred bundle bytes, 588 ms FCP, 812.5 ms DOM interactive, 1,625.9 ms load, zero navigation long tasks, 123 MB host-inclusive heap, and 17.1 ms library mount. The page exposed 180 SharePoint theme entries and 459 Fluent v9 tokens. These are host-inclusive observations from one run, not comparative speed claims. See the [TeachingPopover capture](../test-results/library-demo/teaching-popover.png), [focused interaction data](../test-results/library-demo/teaching-popover-metrics.json), [full tenant metrics](../test-results/library-demo/metrics.json), and [slice benchmarks](component-slice-benchmarks.json).

Tree-shaken slices: native Popover factory 9,939 raw / 3,424 gzip / 2,984 Brotli bytes across 4 modules; React adapter 27,406 / 7,218 / 6,304 bytes across 6 retained modules, with React externalized. This shared native slice includes Popover; it is not an incremental TeachingPopover-only cost. All named APIs are now present in the gap report; the sole missing name is Fluent Virtualizer. TeachingPopover remains partial at behavior and accessibility-parity level. Site-collection catalog reports .112 deployed, enabled, and valid; InstalledVersion remains stale at .35 even though the expected bundle is served. The lifecycle `Upgrade` REST request returned HTTP 200 but did not advance that metadata, so the mismatch is unresolved.



### Virtualizer implementation checkpoint — SPFx package 1.0.0.113

Added a React Virtualizer adapter backed by the existing bounded VirtualList engine. It accepts an item count and index renderer without allocating a synthetic item array, maps virtualizerLength and bufferItems into overscan, reports rendered ranges and scroll state, and supports imperative scroll-to-index and a caller-provided scroll view ref. The SPFx example demonstrates a 5,000-task list and a real “Jump to last task” action.

The authenticated page 058 validator confirmed the expected .113 bundle, bounded rendering (16 rows initially and at the end for 5,000 items), navigation to item 5,000, and no page, console, resource, or profile-photo errors. The jump interaction measured 258 ms including Playwright/page scheduling. The cache-disabled page observation measured 386,958 decoded / 125,055 encoded / 125,355 transferred bundle bytes, 452 ms FCP, 692.1 ms DOM interactive, 3,512.2 ms load, zero navigation long tasks, 131 MB host-inclusive heap, and 18.0 ms library mount. The page exposed 180 SharePoint theme entries and 459 Fluent v9 tokens. This is one host-inclusive run, not a comparative speed claim. See [Virtualizer screenshot](../test-results/library-demo/virtual-list.png), [full tenant metrics](../test-results/library-demo/metrics.json), and [slice benchmarks](component-slice-benchmarks.json).

Tree-shaken slices: native factory 4,623 raw / 1,792 gzip / 1,516 Brotli bytes across 3 modules; React adapter 19,502 / 4,909 / 4,327 bytes across 3 retained modules with React externalized. The App Catalog reports .113 deployed, enabled, and valid, and the exact hashed bundle is served. InstalledVersion remains .35, so metadata divergence remains unresolved. The fixed-height vertical adapter is a useful index-renderer API but does not establish full Fluent Virtualizer parity: dynamic-height, horizontal, browser-viewport, and the broader contributed hook/scroll-view APIs remain gaps.


### DataGrid column reordering checkpoint — SPFx package 1.0.0.115

Added opt-in column reordering to the React DataGrid. Users can drag a dedicated grip or focus it and use Alt+Shift+ArrowLeft/ArrowRight; direction follows the grid's computed LTR/RTL direction. Controlled and default order props allow applications to save and restore the layout, and a polite status region announces the moved column and position. The SPFx example uses controlled order state and Arabic labels. Fixing the new header layout also corrected a specificity bug: the generic header-button width rule had expanded the reorder grip and reduced sort buttons to 12px. The visual capture now shows all four column names and distinct, padded reorder grips.

SPFx 1.24 package .115 passed on the authenticated page 058. For 5,000 rows, 11 rows mounted initially and 12 at the end; sorting, selection, pointer/keyboard resize, double-click auto-fit, keyboard/pointer reorder, and controlled order updates passed. Reorder checks observed id/title/owner/state → id/title/state/owner by keyboard, then id/owner/title/state by pointer; the status announcement was “Status, 3 of 4.” Jump-to-last measured 107 ms including page scheduling. The cache-disabled run served the exact .115 bundle (389,642 decoded / 126,109 encoded / 126,409 transferred bytes); FCP 660 ms, DOM interactive 657.8 ms, load 3,453 ms, zero navigation long tasks, 177 MB host-inclusive heap, and 18.0 ms library mount. It exposed 180 SharePoint theme entries and 459 Fluent v9 tokens, with zero page, console, resource, or profile-photo errors. This is one host-inclusive measurement, not a comparative speed claim. See the [DataGrid capture](../test-results/library-demo/data-grid.png), [full tenant metrics](../test-results/library-demo/metrics.json), and [benchmark ledger](component-slice-benchmarks.json).

DataGrid still needs comparison of its full API/state behavior against Fluent and stronger state/accessibility coverage. The full suite remains partial; this checkpoint closes column reordering and row grouping, not the whole family.


### DataGrid parity checkpoint — SPFx package 1.0.0.117

Column ordering now works in both the React adapter and framework-neutral custom element. React exposes opt-in reorderableColumns, controlled columnOrder, defaultColumnOrder, onColumnOrderChange, and a localized handle label. The custom element exposes a reorderable-columns attribute/property, columnOrder property, and bubbling spui-column-order-change event. Both support pointer drag and Alt+Shift+Arrow movement, logical RTL direction, and an accessible live announcement. A verification run found and fixed forward-move no-ops: moving after the adjacent column now inserts after it, and unchanged orders emit no callback/event. Header layout specificity was also corrected so sort labels remain visible beside the grip.

The authenticated SPFx 1.24 page 058 passed React and custom-element order changes, RTL keyboard movement, controlled/property order, and event delivery. Existing 5,000-row sort, selection, resize, auto-fit, and bounded virtualization checks also passed (11 initial rows, 12 at the end); jump to the last item took 157 ms including page scheduling. The exact .117 bundle served 412,728 decoded / 133,993 encoded / 134,293 transferred bytes; FCP 400 ms, DOM interactive 621.6 ms, load 3,319.5 ms, zero navigation long tasks, 123 MB host-inclusive heap, and 25 ms library mount. The page exposed 180 SharePoint theme entries and 459 Fluent v9 tokens. One run recorded two pageerror payloads; a separate plain-navigation reproduction identified them as PlaywrightError objects with empty name, stack, and log and message “undefined,” before any component interaction. Console, resource, and profile-photo errors were zero. Their source is unattributed; they are not counted as a component regression. App Catalog reports .117 deployed/enabled/valid, but InstalledVersion remains .35 and installed is false, so that metadata mismatch remains unresolved. See the [DataGrid capture](../test-results/library-demo/data-grid.png), [full tenant metrics](../test-results/library-demo/metrics.json), and [component benchmark ledger](component-slice-benchmarks.json).

Tree-shaken slices are 24,052 raw / 6,745 gzip / 6,007 Brotli bytes for the custom-element API and 30,477 / 8,521 / 7,595 bytes for the React adapter (React externalized; 4 retained modules each). Compared with the prior measured DataGrid slices, this adds 1,206 gzip / 1,099 Brotli bytes to the native slice and 1,318 / 1,172 bytes to React. These are import costs, not a browser performance comparison. At this checkpoint, row grouping, multi-sort/filter, and editable cells were still open; later sections record their implementation. Remaining DataGrid work is broad behavior/state/accessibility comparison.

### DataGrid row grouping checkpoint — SPFx package 1.0.0.122

Added shared stable group keys and grouping by any data column. React exposes `groupBy`, controlled/default collapsed group keys, localized expand/collapse and empty-group labels, and a state callback. The custom element exposes `group-by`, `collapsedGroupKeys`, and a bubbling `spui-group-state-change` event. Group headings include accessible row-header semantics, expanded state, and item counts. They share the virtualized row stream, while selection remains item-based; grouping results are cached in the custom element so scrolling does not regroup all records. Arrow navigation skips group rows and stays on a data row at the first/last boundary. The SPFx example includes a grouping selector for Status, Owner, or none.

The authenticated SPFx 1.24 page 058 passed the full tenant validator on `.122`, serving the exact `library-demo-web-part_dfec1d00e73e9a68571f.js` bundle (420,370 decoded / 136,654 encoded bytes; 136,954 transferred). Grouping produced 3 groups across 5,000 records (5,003 display rows); React and native collapse/expand and native state-event delivery passed. The grid mounted 10 rows initially and 12 at the end; jump-to-last took 164 ms. Pointer resize (171 → 120 px), keyboard resize (120 → 130 px), auto-fit (448 → 220 px), keyboard/pointer reorder, and RTL movement passed. The navigation capture recorded FCP 688 ms, DOM interactive 690.4 ms, load 3,364.7 ms, zero navigation long tasks, and 103 MB browser heap. The full page workload recorded 3 long tasks and a 21 ms library mount; page, console, and resource errors were zero. One tenant visit is an observation, not a percentile or comparative speed claim.

Final tree-shaken DataGrid slices are 28,487 raw / 7,853 gzip / 6,993 Brotli bytes for the custom-element API and 33,449 / 9,341 / 8,337 bytes for the React adapter with React externalized (4 included modules). The benchmark excludes the React runtime, CSS, and SharePoint host. Adding grouping cost 1,108 gzip / 986 Brotli bytes to the previous custom-element slice and 820 / 742 bytes to React. The app catalog reports `.122` deployed, enabled, and valid; SharePoint still reports InstalledVersion `.35` and `installed: false`. This metadata mismatch persists independently of the loaded bundle, which the run verified at the exact `.122` asset path. See the [ungrouped DataGrid capture](../test-results/library-demo/data-grid.png), [grouped DataGrid capture](../test-results/library-demo/data-grid-grouped.png), [tenant metrics](../test-results/library-demo/metrics.json), and [component benchmark ledger](component-slice-benchmarks.json).

Validation lesson: when adding custom-element or React style rules, preserve the existing base stylesheet and append new component-state rules. A tenant hit-test on the resize handle caught an accidental base-style replacement (zero-height handle, no pointer delivery) before release. Grouped virtual lists only mount nearby group headings; use their accessible row count for total group counts instead of querying every heading from the DOM. These checks are retained in `scripts/tenant-validate.mjs`.

### DataGrid cell editing checkpoint — SPFx package 1.0.0.125

React and custom-element DataGrid adapters now support opt-in editable columns with text, number, and date editors; Enter/F2 entry; Enter/blur commit; Escape cancel; synchronous validation; async save errors; and a commit callback/event only after success. Local and tenant runs caught a native editor focus bug during virtualized repaint; focus and selection now survive internal row replacement without accidentally committing. The sample keeps persistence host-owned and demonstrates a React state update.

Local Chromium checks passed for both adapters: keyboard entry, validation, text/number commit, async failure retention, and cancel; the native path also retained focus across repaint. Tree-shaken JS is 38,519 raw / 10,349 gzip / 9,262 Brotli bytes for the custom element and 39,977 / 11,495 / 10,289 bytes for React (React externalized; 4 retained modules). Compared with the preceding grouped slice, editing adds 2,496 gzip / 2,269 Brotli bytes to the native entry and 2,154 / 1,952 bytes to React. These sizes exclude CSS, React runtime, and SharePoint host.

On authenticated page 059, the exact `.125` bundle passed keyboard entry, required-title validation, state commit, and restoration of the original sample value. The grid contains 5,000 items and initially mounted 11 rows. The first commit observation was 12.7 ms; a repeat after lifecycle operations was 12.2 ms. Both include Playwright/page scheduling. The bundle resource was 451,485 decoded / 148,629 encoded / 148,929 transferred bytes (627.5 ms on the repeat). There were no page or console errors. This sample did not write to a SharePoint list. The site-collection catalog reports `.125` deployed/enabled/valid; the tenant catalog reports `.124` for the same ProductId. Both have blank `InstalledVersion`, and the site instance is `Initialized` (status `9`), not Installed (status `5`). The ALM helper now identifies status names, invokes Upgrade when a site instance exists despite blank version metadata, and surfaces action failures promptly. A guarded uninstall/install replacement generated a new instance but it also remained Initialized; Upgrade returned HTTP 200 without advancing the state, and Install on the existing instance returned HTTP 400 (“An instance of this App already exists at the specified location”). This metadata issue is unresolved; the page continued serving and passing the `.125` runtime check. See the [local edit capture](../test-results/data-grid-edit-local.png), [tenant edit capture](../test-results/library-demo/data-grid-edit.png), and [benchmark ledger](component-slice-benchmarks.json).

Dynamic data sources and batch/clipboard editing were still open at this checkpoint; later sections record their implementation and local verification. Remaining DataGrid work is broad Fluent behavior/state/accessibility comparison, including assistive-technology and current authenticated SharePoint validation.

### DataGrid horizontal column windowing checkpoint — local browser validation

React and custom-element DataGrid now keep the complete header and grid tracks for sizing, sorting, reordering, resize controls, and horizontal scrolling, while mounting only the visible body columns plus two columns of overscan. Both adapters retain full `aria-colcount` and logical `aria-colindex`, and keyboard End scrolls to and focuses the final column in LTR and RTL. The native adapter now restores focus after the scroll-triggered virtual-row repaint so horizontal keyboard navigation does not leave focus behind.

Chromium headless validation used 100 columns × 500 records in a 760 px browser viewport with 120 px columns. It mounted at most 9 data cells per row (visible columns plus overscan) instead of 100, a 91% reduction in per-row cell count. The React grid mounted 99 body cells across 11 virtualized rows; the native element mounted 54 across 6 rows. Both retained `aria-colcount="100"`, exposed the last column as `aria-colindex="100"`, and passed LTR/RTL End-navigation checks. This measures DOM cell count, not interaction latency or SharePoint-host cost. The current standalone DataGrid slices measure 10,846 B gzip / 9,745 B Brotli for the custom element and 12,092 B / 10,830 B for React (React externalized; 4 retained modules each). Dynamic data sources and batch/clipboard editing were open at this checkpoint and have since been implemented; broad behavior/state/accessibility comparison remains.

### Variable-height virtualization checkpoint — local package 0.4.0

VirtualList supports opt-in measured item sizes through `measure-items` / `measureItems`; React Virtualizer delegates imperative index navigation to the shared list engine. A Fenwick prefix-sum index provides O(log n) offset lookup and size updates, with O(n) initialization. Rows retain bounded viewport-plus-overscan rendering, and scroll anchoring compensates for measured changes before the visible range. The fixed-size path remains the default. Corrected the native default overscan handling so an omitted attribute uses four rows instead of coercing `null` to zero.

Local Chromium with 5,000 vertical rows mounted 11 initially and 13 at the end; measured heights included 40 px and 109 px, and the last row was present after index navigation. Horizontal and RTL behavior is recorded in the following checkpoint. Synchronous jump dispatch measured 0.3 ms, excluding paint/layout settling. A synthetic 200,000 lookup/update workload for 5,000 estimated/measured items took 12.2 ms in one Node run (0.06 µs per operation average; not a UI latency claim). SPFx 1.24 build and catalog validation pass. This change has not been deployed to SharePoint; tenant runtime remains pending because the active page serves an earlier bundle hash. See `scripts/benchmark-variable-virtual-list.mjs` and [benchmark ledger](component-slice-benchmarks.json).

### Horizontal and RTL Virtualizer checkpoint — local Chromium validation

React `VirtualList`/`Virtualizer` and framework-neutral `spui-virtual-list` accept `axis="horizontal"` as well as the vertical default. `itemSize` (or native `item-size`) is the estimated extent along that axis; `measureItems` updates measured widths or heights through the shared Fenwick size index. Scroll-to-index, alignment, viewport observation, overscan, and scroll anchoring use the active axis. RTL uses the logical start edge and normalizes the browser's negative `scrollLeft` values.

Chromium rendered 6/5,000 React items and 8/5,000 native items initially in 360 px viewports. Both adapters navigated to a visibly intersecting item 5,000 in LTR and RTL; RTL measured-width mode used negative scroll offsets and reported distinct item extents. Vertical mode also stayed bounded and reached its last visible item. No browser errors occurred. These checks verify bounded DOM count, position semantics, measured sizes, and RTL scrolling, not touch/trackpad latency or browser-page viewport integration. SPFx 1.24 production package compilation and catalog validation pass; no tenant package has been published. Updated VirtualList slices are 2,987 B gzip / 2,623 B Brotli for the custom element and 6,156 B / 5,430 B for React; the Virtualizer adapter is 2,993 B / 2,624 B for its native definition and 6,662 B / 5,897 B for React. These exclude React runtime, CSS, and SharePoint host costs. See `scripts/verify-virtualizer-axis.mjs` and [slice ledger](component-slice-benchmarks.json).

### Native TeachingPopover parity checkpoint

Added the framework-neutral `defineTeachingPopover()` composition alongside the React family. It registers the guided Popover and trigger/surface/header/title/body/footer parts, plus an accessible carousel with localized page labels/counts, RTL keyboard movement, roving navigation, edge-aware footer labels, and focus return. The slot traversal fix makes focus restoration work when the actual trigger is assigned through a display-contents custom-element wrapper. Local Playwright validation passed both React and native APIs in Arabic/RTL with zero page errors.

Measured minified tree-shaken slices: native factory 26,549 raw / 7,137 gzip / 6,319 Brotli bytes (5 retained modules); React adapter 44,818 / 11,119 / 9,804 bytes (7 retained modules, React externalized). These are import-size measures, not runtime performance claims. Catalog validation passes for 105 entries and the name-only report finds 0/51 Fluent families missing a name; behavioral, accessibility, theme, and performance parity remain open. The native implementation has local browser evidence only; earlier React tenant evidence does not validate the native API in SPFx.

### RichTextEditor table editing checkpoint — local SPFx 1.24 build

Added localized toolbar controls for inserting a row above/below and a column before/after the selected table cell, deleting the selected row/column, and removing the table. Controls remain disabled outside a selected cell and for merged-cell tables, avoiding ambiguous span rewrites. The editor retains its active table-cell position across toolbar focus and sanitizer DOM replacement; deleting a column now captures its containing row before removing the selected cell, avoiding a detached-cell exception.

Focused Chromium validation passed native custom-element and React editing flows, Arabic accessible labels, merged-cell guards, sanitization, and zero browser errors. The SPFx 1.24 production package build passes. Tree-shaken JS sizes are 15,409 raw / 4,814 gzip / 4,207 Brotli bytes for the custom element and 34,096 / 8,861 / 7,741 bytes for the React adapter (React externalized). The latest implementation is locally validated only; SharePoint save/readback for the added table commands remains to be exercised after deployment.

### Toast/Toaster controller, keyboard, and announcement checkpoint — local SPFx 1.24 validation

Added `pauseToast(id)`/`playToast(id)` to the React `useToastController()` API and `pause(id)`/`play(id)` to the framework-neutral Toaster. Focusing any toast pauses all toast timers in the region; ArrowUp/ArrowDown moves through visible toasts; Delete dismisses the focused toast and restores focus; Escape retains focused-or-newest dismissal. `timeout: 0` and Fluent-compatible `timeout: -1` both mean persistent. The Toaster now owns one polite and one assertive live region, serializes announcements, prioritizes assertive items over pending polite items, and suppresses new announcements while focus is in the region. Individual toasts are groups, avoiding competing live regions.

Local Chromium checks passed timer pause/resume, group pause while focused, expiry after focus leaves, persistent timeout semantics, newest/adjacent focus, Delete focus restoration, group semantics in both adapters, and announcement ordering `polite one → assertive one → assertive two → polite two`. The SPFx 1.24 production build passes. Current isolated tree-shaken slices: Toast is 3,185 B gzip / 2,748 B Brotli native and 7,237 B / 6,284 B React; Toaster is 4,942 B / 4,337 B native and 9,533 B / 8,324 B React. These minified JS slices exclude CSS assets, React runtime, and SharePoint host overhead. Fluent's Toast implementation uses one current announcement, priority queues, stable polite/assertive live regions, and disables announcements while focus is inside the Toaster ([announcement queue](https://github.com/microsoft/fluentui/blob/master/packages/react-components/react-toast/library/src/components/AriaLive/useAriaLive.ts), [focus suppression](https://github.com/microsoft/fluentui/blob/master/packages/react-components/react-toast/library/src/components/Toaster/useToastAnnounce.ts)). Chromium validated DOM updates and ordering, not speech output from an actual screen reader. Screen-reader/tenant validation and family-wide visual contrast/state comparisons remain open.


### Nested Menu keyboard parity checkpoint — local SPFx 1.24 validation

Menu now treats a nested Menu trigger as one parent-menu item. In LTR the Right Arrow opens a submenu; in RTL the Left Arrow opens it. The opposite arrow closes one level and restores focus to its trigger. Submenus default to logical-end placement, expose menu-haspopup/expanded semantics on their trigger, and focus their first enabled item. Escape closes one submenu level, Tab closes the menu hierarchy, and an outside pointer press dismisses all open levels. Menu surfaces use manual popovers so opening a child does not dismiss its parent.

Chromium checks passed for the React adapter in LTR and framework-neutral custom elements in RTL: arrow opening, first-item focus, return focus, parent continuity, per-level Escape, hierarchy dismissal by Tab/outside click, trigger semantics, and logical placement. There were zero browser errors. Minified tree-shaken slices measure 4,010 B gzip / 3,516 B Brotli for custom elements and 7,995 B / 6,957 B for the React adapter; React runtime, CSS assets, and SharePoint host costs are excluded. SPFx 1.24 production package and component catalog checks pass. Nested submenu tenant behavior remains pending the SharePoint app-instance lifecycle issue described below.

### MenuBar extension — local Chromium checkpoint

Added an optional horizontal `MenuBar` pattern that composes the shared Menu engine. This is an additive navigation pattern, not a separately named Fluent v9 catalog family. The React LTR and framework-neutral RTL Chromium checks passed: accessible menubar label/orientation, a single Tab stop, Home/End, direction-aware horizontal movement, opening a top-level menu with ArrowDown, menu focus, Escape focus return, sibling-menu coordination, outside dismissal, and Arabic labeling. Existing nested submenu checks were rerun successfully. Zero browser errors were observed.

Tree-shaken MenuBar slices: custom-element entry 20,492 B raw / 5,227 B gzip / 4,604 B Brotli (3 modules); React adapter 37,998 B / 8,976 B / 7,863 B (5 retained modules, React externalized). These local bundle measurements exclude CSS, runtime dependencies, and SharePoint host costs. MenuBar itself has not been tenant-validated: SharePoint still serves a stale asset despite accepting package 1.0.0.133; see the deployment checkpoint below.

Fluent Menu source comparison found first-character navigation wraps from the focused item and repeated letters cycle among matching items. The local Menu had accumulated those letters into a prefix, so repeated-key cycling failed. The shared engine now wraps forward for first-character and repeated-character search while retaining its additional multi-character prefix search. The focused React and framework-neutral verifiers pass these paths, RTL/LTR nested menus, focus restoration, persistence, and zero browser errors. The MenuBar regression run also exposed that its key handler could not identify a focused menu item through shadow DOM; it now resolves the owning item from the composed event path. The React LTR and native RTL MenuBar checks pass Home/End, direction-aware movement, submenu opening, Escape restoration, and sibling dismissal. Current Menu slices are 30,032 raw / 7,246 gzip / 6,380 Brotli bytes for custom elements and 55,765 / 13,481 / 11,851 for React (React externalized; 5 retained modules). These are local tree-shaken JavaScript estimates, excluding CSS, React runtime, and SharePoint host costs; no tenant or assistive-technology validation was run.

### ContextMenu invocation — local Chromium checkpoint

The existing `Menu` engine now supports a context-menu trigger mode through the React `ContextMenu` adapter or the framework-neutral `context-menu` attribute. Right-click opens at the pointer; the Context Menu key and Shift+F10 open aligned to the trigger. Each route moves focus to the first enabled item, Escape restores trigger focus, and an outside pointer press dismisses. RTL keyboard placement anchors at the logical start edge. Chromium checks passed for React LTR and native RTL with zero page errors; nested-menu and MenuBar regression checks also pass. Assistive-technology speech and tenant runtime remain unverified while SharePoint serves a stale bundle.

Tree-shaken Menu import: custom-element core 14,770 B raw / 4,261 B gzip / 3,722 B Brotli (3 modules); React adapter 32,831 B / 8,264 B / 7,208 B (5 retained modules, React externalized). ContextMenu adds a React wrapper; its import is 14,770 B / 4,261 B / 3,722 B for custom elements and 32,893 B / 8,282 B / 7,213 B for React. The combined Menu and ContextMenu imports are 14,774 B / 4,263 B / 3,722 B and 32,895 B / 8,284 B / 7,217 B, respectively. React runtime, CSS, and SharePoint host costs are excluded. SPFx 1.24 production build and 108-entry catalog check pass; the current build has not been published.

Tenant package 1.0.0.133 was deployed to the correct tenant catalog and reported valid/enabled/deployed. The one authenticated page check still served the older e70d3c0908a27188944d bundle; InstalledVersion is blank and its site instance is still Initialized (9, InError false). The lifecycle helper did not repeat Install or Upgrade. This remains a deployment-state blocker for tenant verification, not evidence against the local Menu behavior. Diagnostic capture: test-results/library-demo/deployment-diagnostic.json.

### DataGrid variable-height row checkpoint — local Chromium validation

React and framework-neutral DataGrid now accept `getRowHeight(item, index)` in addition to the fixed `rowHeight`/`row-height` estimate. Heights are clamped to 1–10,000 px, item rows use record-specific sizes, and group rows use the estimate. The shared Fenwick index now supports linear-time initialization from per-row sizes; virtual range lookup, spacer extents, row positioning, keyboard reveal, and total scroll height use prefix sums. The native renderer keeps a cached index for a stable displayed-row set so scrolling does not rebuild it.

Chromium checks passed with 500 rows, 100 columns, and mixed 24/40/64 px heights: React mounted 10 rows initially and the native element 6, with at most 9 cells per row due to column virtualization. Scroll extents matched expected content (20,048 px React; 20,027 px native, including measured header heights), keyboard movement advanced into the second row, end scrolling mounted row 500, and RTL End navigation still reached column 100. No browser errors occurred. Tree-shaken DataGrid slices are 42,375 raw / 11,533 gzip / 10,367 Brotli bytes for the native element and 43,356 / 12,661 / 11,305 bytes for React (React externalized; 5 retained modules). These estimates exclude CSS, React runtime, and SharePoint host. See `scripts/verify-data-grid-horizontal.mjs` and `investigation/component-slice-benchmarks.json`. Tenant deployment has not been run for this checkpoint.

### DataGrid clipboard and batch edit checkpoint — local Chromium validation

DataGrid now copies the focused cell or selected rows as quoted TSV and pastes rectangular TSV into editable cells. Clipboard parsing preserves quoted tabs, embedded newlines, doubled quotes, and CRLF. Paste validates the full target range (editable columns, bounds, numeric/date values, and consumer validation) before any write, with a 1,000-cell limit. A single-cell paste uses `onCellEdit`; multi-cell paste requires `onCellsEdit`, which the host must persist atomically. Local values update only after the callback succeeds; rejection leaves the complete range unchanged. React and native adapters expose `spui-cells-edit` and `spui-cells-edit-error` events, plus an accessible live status. The SPFx example now demonstrates bulk state persistence and includes English/Arabic clipboard instructions.

Chromium passed selected-row copy and quoted multi-cell paste through both adapters, including numeric conversion, read-only target rejection, callback rejection without partial local updates, and zero browser errors. Model checks cover escaped quotes, embedded tabs/newlines, trailing empty cells, and invalid numbers. Tree-shaken slices are 46,458 raw / 12,734 gzip / 11,404 Brotli bytes for the custom element and 46,568 / 13,757 / 12,281 bytes for the React adapter (React externalized; 5 retained modules). These exclude CSS, React runtime, and SharePoint host costs. See `scripts/verify-data-grid-clipboard.mjs` and `investigation/component-slice-benchmarks.json`. Tenant deployment remains pending.

### Disabled Field label state and package lifecycle checkpoint

React `Field` and the framework-neutral `createFormField` now mark labels disabled when either the wrapper or control is disabled, including native disabled-fieldset inheritance. React server rendering and local Chromium checks passed for explicit wrapper/control disablement, disabled fieldsets, and enabled controls. Library TypeScript build and the 108-entry component catalog check pass.

The `.134` tenant runtime and the previous version-reporting mismatch therefore remain unresolved. Post-deploy evidence: `test-results/library-demo/deployment-diagnostic.json`.

### ColorPicker HSV controls — local SPFx 1.24 package 1.0.0.135

Added an interactive saturation/brightness area and hue slider to the existing hex/native color/opacity/swatches control. Pointer selection and arrow keys update the same hex value and `spui-change` event; horizontal arrows respect RTL, Shift+Arrow uses larger steps, and Home/End set saturation bounds. The React and custom-element paths share the behavior, labels and live value text are localizable, and disabled/read-only/form states are reflected. Added reflected `name`, `disabled`, `required`, and `readOnly` properties for normal form-associated custom-element use.

Chromium passed controlled React keyboard/hue changes, native pointer selection, Arabic labels/value text, RTL arrow behavior, alpha form serialization, disabled behavior, and zero page errors at 390×844. ### ColorPicker HEX/RGB entry — local SPFx 1.24 package 1.0.0.136

Added an accessible HEX/RGB format selector. ### Toast visible limits and priority queue — local Chromium

Added Fluent-style `Toaster limit` and per-Toast `priority` across the framework-neutral and React APIs. Excess Toasts remain hidden with timers paused; the lowest numeric priority is promoted first when space opens, with equal-priority FIFO order. Updating a queued Toast's priority changes the next promotion. Queued notifications are announced only when they become visible. Raising or lowering `limit`, direct dismissal, and dismiss-all cover queued as well as visible records. Toast IDs use an internal map and visible/queued membership sets to keep imperative dispatch and limit checks bounded.

Chromium passed limit enforcement, priority/FIFO promotion, queued timer pause, priority updates, queue clearing, live limit changes, serialized announcements, and zero page errors through both adapters. Five local queue-enqueue samples measured p50/p95 at 4.1/5.6 ms for 100 Toasts and 15.1/28.5 ms for 1,000 Toasts, with only five visible in both cases. Tree-shaken Toast+Toaster slices are 19,576 raw / 5,493 gzip / 4,836 Brotli bytes for custom elements and 39,638 / 10,255 / 8,967 bytes for React (React externalized; six retained modules). SPFx 1.24 solution 1.0.0.140 / feature 1.0.0.76 builds; `.sppkg` is 122,713 bytes and the demo bundle is 507,390 bytes. These exclude CSS, React runtime, and SharePoint host cost. See `scripts/verify-toast-parity.mjs` and the [benchmark ledger](component-slice-benchmarks.json). Screen-reader speech and authenticated tenant verification remain open.

Existing picker behavior retains its fallback area, hue and alpha controls when no children are supplied.

`ColorArea`, `ColorSlider`, and `AlphaSlider` now accept `color`/`defaultColor` HSV state and call `onChange(event, { color })` when used outside a `ColorPicker`. The framework-neutral elements expose corresponding `color` and `defaultColor` properties. Inside a picker, the controls continue to share its HSV state and their own callbacks receive the changed HSV object.

### VirtualList async range source — local Chromium and SPFx 1.24

React `VirtualList` and framework-neutral `spui-virtual-list` now accept an indexed source (`length`, synchronous `getItem`, abortable end-exclusive `loadRange`, and optional `subscribe`). Each adapter requests only its rendered window, cancels stale requests when the viewport moves, exposes busy state, and reports failures through a React callback or `spui-data-load-error`. Resolving a range with unloaded slots is reported as an error; source notifications can retry the current range. Existing array-based list use is unchanged.

Chromium passed initial load, scroll cancellation, successful range hydration, rejected and incomplete loads, subscription retry, accessible list positions, and zero page errors. With 5,000 items, the React list mounted 9 rows and the native list 13 (99.82% and 99.74% fewer rows than eager rendering). The local synthetic 80 ms source delay validates async lifecycle only; it is not a component latency measure. Tree-shaken slices are 11,210 raw / 3,588 gzip / 3,150 Brotli bytes for custom elements and 24,440 / 6,706 / 5,924 bytes for React (React externalized; 4 retained modules). Async loading adds 601/550 gzip bytes and 527/494 Brotli bytes to the respective fixed-list imports. SPFx 1.24 package 1.0.0.144 / feature 1.0.0.80 builds locally; `.sppkg` is 123,099 bytes and the demo bundle is 508,397 bytes. It was not uploaded; the tenant's status-9 app-instance issue remains unresolved. See `scripts/verify-virtual-list-data-source.mjs`, `scripts/verify-virtualizer-axis.mjs`, and the [benchmark ledger](component-slice-benchmarks.json). ### DataGrid focus modes — local Chromium and SPFx 1.24

Added Fluent-compatible `focusMode` values `none`, `cell` (default), `row_unstable`, and `composite` to React and the native custom element. `none` removes managed cell/row tab stops while keeping nested controls in normal Tab order. Row modes expose one roving row stop; composite mode supports ArrowRight entry, ArrowLeft return, and vertical row movement. The native adapter reflects this through the `focus-mode` attribute and `focusMode` property.

Local Chromium checks passed for both adapters: default cell roving focus, `none` with naturally tabbable nested buttons, active row stops and movement, composite entry/exit/vertical navigation, existing 100-column LTR/RTL virtualization, editing, clipboard, catalog signature validation, and zero page errors. SPFx 1.24 package 1.0.0.145 / feature 1.0.0.81 builds locally; `.sppkg` is 123,836 bytes and the demo bundle is 512,067 bytes. Tree-shaken DataGrid slices are 48,537 raw / 13,154 gzip / 11,778 Brotli bytes for custom elements and 48,534 / 14,182 / 12,687 for React (React externalized; 5 retained modules). The tenant catalog now has .145 deployed/enabled/valid, but the existing site app instance remains Initialized (status 9), `InstalledVersion` is empty, web part registration is missing, and the published page still serves the prior `.138` bundle. One guarded `AppInstance.Install()` retry returned job `fb4fa657-3dbc-4029-bc2f-9757bd3cf3de` but did not transition the instance; no further install attempt was made. See `scripts/verify-data-grid-focus-mode.mjs` and `investigation/component-slice-benchmarks.json`. DataGrid async sources, broader family parity, screen-reader verification, and tenant runtime behavior remain open.

Added row-click and Space selection, excluding nested interactive controls. React and native DataGrid now expose `selectionAppearance` (`brand`, `neutral`, or `none`) and `subtleSelection`; selected surfaces derive from SharePoint accent/text/surface tokens, and subtle selection reveals checkboxes on hover or focus. Focus modes, 100-column LTR/RTL virtualization, editing, clipboard, catalog signatures, and zero-page-error regressions passed locally.

The SPFx 1.24 production build passes at component 0.0.28 / solution 1.0.0.146 / feature 1.0.0.82. The `.sppkg` is 124,610 bytes and demo bundle 516,443 bytes. The tenant was not updated: its installed site instance remains at status 9 on catalog .145. See `scripts/verify-data-grid-focus-mode.mjs` and `investigation/component-slice-benchmarks.json`. Column sizing contract details, async DataGrid sources, broader behavior/accessibility parity, and tenant runtime verification remain open.

### DataGrid column sizing configuration — local Chromium and SPFx 1.24

React accepts Fluent-shaped `columnSizingOptions` and `resizableColumnsOptions.autoFitColumns`; the native element exposes `columnSizingOptions` and `autoFitColumns`. Per-column `defaultWidth`, `idealWidth`, `minWidth`, and `padding` configure initial sizing, keyboard/pointer bounds, and content auto-fit. Auto-fit fills the last resizable column to the available grid width. Resize updates now retain the native separator node and update its width/ARIA value in place; this also restores actual double-click auto-fit, which had been interrupted by rebuilding the header between pointer releases.

Local Chromium checks passed in both adapters: configured 180px initial width, accessible/enforced 90px minimum, keyboard resize to 100px, double-click auto-fit to 210px, matching callback values, 678px auto-fit column in a 680px viewport, focus/selection regression, and 100-column LTR/RTL virtualization. There were zero browser errors. The SPFx 1.24 package builds at component 0.0.29 / solution 1.0.0.147 / feature 1.0.0.83; `.sppkg` is 125,397 bytes and the demo bundle is 519,170 bytes. Tree-shaken DataGrid slices are 53,078 raw / 14,094 gzip / 12,593 Brotli bytes native and 51,819 / 14,981 / 13,388 bytes React (React externalized; 5 retained modules), adding 468/449 gzip bytes over the selection checkpoint. This checkpoint is local only; tenant verification is still blocked by the pre-existing status-9 app instance. See `scripts/verify-data-grid-sizing.mjs` and `investigation/component-slice-benchmarks.json`. Async DataGrid sources and broader family behavior/accessibility parity remain open.


#### Auto-fit distribution refinement — SPFx solution 1.0.0.148

The initial sizing checkpoint only let the final column absorb remaining space. The shared algorithm now grows tracks toward `idealWidth` in column order, assigns leftover space to the last column, and shrinks from the last column toward each `minWidth`. React and native validation matched at [178, 100, 120]px in a 400px container and [88, 80, 80]px in a 250px container. Tree-shaken slices are 54,872 raw / 14,505 gzip / 12,962 Brotli bytes native and 52,853 / 15,353 / 13,766 bytes React. Solution 1.0.0.148 builds locally; `.sppkg` is 125,924 bytes and the demo bundle is 521,125 bytes. No tenant upload was made.

### DataGrid indexed async source — local Chromium and SPFx 1.24

React `DataGrid` and framework-neutral `spui-data-grid` now accept an indexed `dataSource` with `length`, abortable end-exclusive `loadRange`, optional `subscribe`, and either `getItem` for flat rows or `getRow` for item/group rows. Length counts displayed rows, including group headers; `selectableCount` can report the exact item count when groups are present. Both render a bounded visible window, expose loading rows and busy state, cancel stale range requests, report load failures, and retry after source notification. Providers own server-side sorting, filtering, and grouping; local transforms are bypassed for partial data. Accessible group buttons call `onDataSourceGroupToggle` in React or the native property and dispatch `spui-data-source-group-toggle`; providers update their row sequence and notify subscribers. Indexed selection supports a compact all-matching model with excluded keys, without enumerating remote IDs. A failed request remains visible to assistive technology through a live status message. The fixed-height virtual index now uses O(1) metadata and computes offsets directly; it allocates prefix-sum arrays only when variable row measurements are needed.

Chromium validated a 50,000-row source: React mounted 7 rows and requested [0,7); native mounted 4 and requested [0,4). Across the complete interaction sequence, the source received 458 React and 142 native `getItem` reads, far below a 50,000-row scan. Both adapters cancel stale ranges, announce rejected loads, retry after subscription notification, support accessible provider-owned group expansion, and produce zero page errors. All-matching selection, exclusions, partial checkbox state, clearing, and query-scope reset passed in both adapters. A 10-million-row fixed-height index passed total-height, offset, and index lookup assertions without allocating per-row sizes. Provider clipboard checks passed remote all-selection TSV copy and missing-destination-range paste through both adapters; only the requested paste rows were fetched. Existing array-backed copy/paste, type conversion, invalid target, and failed-transaction atomicity checks also passed. The synthetic 80 ms range delay checks lifecycle behavior, not production interaction latency. Current tree-shaken DataGrid slices are 66,695 raw / 17,097 gzip / 15,172 Brotli bytes native and 60,259 / 17,570 / 15,712 React (React externalized; 5 included modules). The provider clipboard and query-scope additions add 839 gzip bytes native and 821 React compared with the preceding indexed-selection checkpoint. SPFx 1.24 package 0.0.34 / solution 1.0.0.152 / feature 1.0.0.88 builds locally; `.sppkg` is 130,492 bytes and demo bundle is 539,290 bytes. No tenant upload was made because the existing status-9 app instance remains unresolved. Broader DataGrid behavior/accessibility comparison and family-level Fluent parity remain open. See `scripts/verify-data-grid-source.mjs`, `scripts/verify-data-grid-provider-clipboard.mjs`, and `investigation/component-slice-benchmarks.json`.

### Toolbar groups and separators — React/native and SPFx 1.24 local verification

Added `ToolbarGroup` and `ToolbarSeparator` to the React adapter and framework-neutral element API. Groups expose `role="group"` with an optional accessible label; their controls remain in the parent Toolbar's single roving tab sequence. Separators expose `role="separator"` and automatically switch to vertical in horizontal toolbars and horizontal in vertical toolbars, using the theme stroke token and a named `::part(line)` hook. Toolbar now watches nested open shadow roots and defers a coalesced focus rescan to the next animation frame so controls that attach their shadow content after insertion still receive one tab stop.

Chromium passed React and native checks for accessible group names, separator orientation, ArrowRight traversal across group boundaries, disabled-command skipping, `loop={false}`, vertical orientation, RTL movement, and zero page errors. Tree-shaken Toolbar + group + separator slices are 7,895 raw / 2,455 gzip / 2,125 Brotli bytes for custom elements and 26,067 / 6,431 / 5,568 bytes for React (React externalized; 5 included modules). Compared with Toolbar alone, groups and separators add 5 gzip bytes to the custom-element entry and 59 gzip bytes to the React import. The SPFx 1.24 package builds at component 0.0.35 / solution 1.0.0.153 / feature 1.0.0.89; `.sppkg` is 130,997 bytes and the demo bundle is 542,301 bytes. These sizes exclude CSS, React runtime, and SharePoint host costs from the isolated slices. Tenant deployment remains withheld while the existing status-9 app instance is unresolved. `scripts/verify-toolbar-groups.mjs` provides the browser regression check.

### Shared Tooltip and Popover positioning — local Chromium and SPFx 1.24

Tooltip and Popover now share a small collision-aware placement function with logical `start`/`end` and physical side support. It flips to the available side and clamps to viewport edges; Popover arrows follow the resolved placement. Tooltip and Popover surfaces use the browser top layer when available, so transformed or overflow-clipped SharePoint containers do not hide them. The fallback keeps fixed positioning where the Popover API is unavailable.

Chromium at 390×844 passed Tooltip vertical and logical horizontal collision flips, resize repositioning, escape from a transformed clipping container, Popover bottom and RTL-start flips, viewport clamping, arrow direction, and top-layer rendering. TeachingPopover keyboard/focus and DatePicker localization/form regression checks also passed after the shared utility change, all with zero page errors. Isolated tree-shaken slices: Tooltip custom element 6,646 raw / 2,418 gzip / 2,111 Brotli bytes and React 24,912 / 6,475 / 5,645; Popover custom element 11,489 / 3,873 / 3,393 and React 29,881 / 7,980 / 6,978. React is externalized; slices exclude CSS and SharePoint host cost.

SPFx 1.24 production build passed locally at component 0.0.35 / solution 1.0.0.153 / feature 1.0.0.89. Demo bundle `library-demo-web-part_1051dacae79b91c693c1.js` is 584,306 raw / 134,834 gzip / 104,432 Brotli bytes; `.sppkg` is 141,100 bytes. This build was not uploaded to the tenant. The [positioning capture](../test-results/popover-positioning.png) and [benchmark ledger](component-slice-benchmarks.json) contain the reproducible evidence. These targeted checks improve shared overlay behavior but do not establish complete Tooltip or Popover parity; no authenticated SharePoint or screen-reader run was made.

### Optional compact Nav rail — local Chromium and SPFx 1.24

Nav can now opt into a desktop collapse control with `collapsible`. React supports `collapsed`/`onCollapsedChange` and `defaultCollapsed`; the custom element exposes matching `collapsed`/`defaultCollapsed` properties and emits `spui-nav-collapsed-change`. The default expanded desktop width remains 260px. Compact mode is 64px by default and can be styled with `--spui-nav-collapsed-width`; it retains full accessible names, adds hover titles, uses an initial when an item lacks an icon/avatar, and preserves assigned visuals. Expand/collapse labels inherit `navigationExpand` and `navigationCollapse` provider strings. The arrow follows LTR/RTL direction and animates with a short transform; rail width changes immediately to avoid per-frame layout reflow. Reduced motion disables the chevron animation. The 640px mobile menu/drawer continues to use the existing behavior, independent of desktop compact state.

Chromium verified native Space-key activation, React controlled updates, compact/expanded widths, contained item geometry, accessible names, hover titles, avatar and initial fallback, RTL/LTR arrow direction, category disclosure naming/keyboard behavior, reduced-motion chevron behavior, mobile drawer dismissal/focus restoration, and zero page errors. The screenshot review caught and fixed slot-grid min-content overflow so labels/icons stay inside the compact rail. The container width changes immediately to avoid repeated layout during animation; only the chevron transform animates. ### Carousel alignment, appearance, and motion variants — local Chromium and SPFx 1.24

Fade keeps only the active slide focusable and exposed to assistive technology; inactive slides become inert and `aria-hidden`, then recover their original values when fade is disabled or the element disconnects. `motionDuration` is bounded to 0–2000ms; reduced motion removes fade transitions. Elevated surfaces use SharePoint surface, stroke, radius, shadow, and text tokens and switch to system colors in forced-colors mode. The comparison catalog now lists all ten Fluent Carousel exports and reports the ten remaining standalone/provider exports still missing, rather than masking them behind an empty example list.

Added `selectionMode="multiple"`, `selectedValues`, and `defaultSelectedValues` to the framework-neutral element and React adapter. Grid mode exposes `aria-multiselectable`, and row mode uses a labelled group of checkboxes. Roving focus tracks one active swatch independently from selection so multiple selected colors do not add tab stops. Single selection, `selectedValue`, and the original callback fields remain available. The example dashboard now shows a compact multi-color palette beside the original single-select theme picker.

Local Chromium passed native default/controlled selection and deselection, event payloads, grid and row semantics, RTL arrow movement, Space activation, one roving tab stop, React controlled updates, and single-select compatibility with zero page errors. ### Fluent-style composable DataGrid — local Chromium and SPFx 1.24

The React `DataGrid` now has a composition path alongside the existing virtualized `items`/`columns` renderer. It supports `DataGridHeader`, `DataGridBody`, `DataGridRow`, `DataGridHeaderCell`, `DataGridCell`, `DataGridSelectionCell`, and matching context providers. `createTableColumn` definitions drive row render helpers. The composed grid exposes controlled/default selected item sets, controlled/default sort state, select-all, semantic row/column indices, Fluent `none`/`cell`/`row_unstable`/`composite` focus modes, and RTL-aware arrow navigation. The virtualized DataGrid path remains selected when the root has no children.

Playwright passed header/body/item rendering, sorting state and callback, controlled multi-selection and select-all, all four focus modes, composite group entry/exit, row navigation, RTL navigation, existing virtualized focus/selection behavior, and zero browser errors. The composed path now accepts `resizableColumns`, `columnSizingOptions`, controlled/default widths, `onColumnResize`, and `resizableColumnsOptions.autoFitColumns`; shared tracks support pointer/keyboard resizing and double-click visible-content auto-fit. It also supports controlled/default `columnOrder`, `onColumnOrderChange`, native drag-and-drop, and Alt+Shift+Arrow keyboard movement (RTL-aware). Header and body cell placements and ARIA indices follow the same order. The SPFx example uses `helpers.columns` so custom row composition can follow the moved order. Local Chromium verifies drag and keyboard reorder, track/index consistency, RTL movement, resize behavior, and earlier composition checks. Latest DataGrid import measures 66,781 raw / 17,134 gzip / 15,236 Brotli bytes for custom elements and 66,371 / 19,543 / 17,285 for React (five included modules; React externalized); JavaScript only, excluding CSS/runtime/SharePoint. The SPFx 1.24 package was rebuilt locally at solution 1.0.0.159 / feature 1.0.0.95; `.sppkg` is 166,484 bytes and the demo bundle is 705,131 raw / 161,243 gzip / 123,266 Brotli bytes. No tenant deployment, authenticated runtime, screen-reader session, or interaction-latency measurement was done. The composed path remains non-virtualized and lacks editing and asynchronous ranges. Fluent feature hooks and `noNativeElements` behavior also remain open. See `scripts/verify:data-grid-parity`, `scripts/verify-data-grid-composition.mjs`, `scripts/verify-data-grid-focus-mode.mjs`, and the [benchmark ledger](component-slice-benchmarks.json).

### Fluent Popover focus modes and inline composition — local Chromium and SPFx 1.24

PopoverTrigger supports `disableButtonEnhancement` while preserving pre-existing ARIA attributes, and Popover now follows Fluent's default surface autofocus with `unstable_disableAutoFocus` as an opt-out. `trapFocus` supports the legacy Tab/Shift+Tab cycle; `inertTrapFocus` makes outside document content inert and restores the prior inert values on close. Fluent's `legacyTrapFocus={false}` selects that inert mode. The inert coordinator supports simultaneous popovers and preserves focusable content inside each surface. `inline` is reflected in React and custom-element APIs. The custom-element engine always retains slotted content in source order (with `inline` as an explicit intent marker) and uses the browser top layer for unclipped rendering rather than creating a body portal, preserving inherited SharePoint section tokens. Focused Chromium checks pass across React and native APIs, including ARIA preservation, autofocus opt-out, both focus models, concurrent inert overlays and restoration, inline reflection, top-layer rendering, and zero page errors. The prop audit no longer reports any Popover own-prop candidates; catalog validation passes all 219 entries. ### Popover positioning fallbacks and Virtualizer recheck — 2026-11-01

Extended Popover's positioning object with ordered `fallbackPositions`, Fluent auto-sizing modes, `matchTargetSize`, and `pinned`, including logical `before`/`after` sides and alignment suffixes. Placement selects the first candidate that fits every viewport edge, then uses the automatic opposite-side fallback and viewport clamp; pinned mode skips flipping, and if no candidate fully fits the engine selects the one with least overflow. Fallback alignment applies on the cross axis. Auto-sizing constrains width and/or height to available space and makes oversized surfaces scrollable. React and custom-element paths preserve the same positioning object. Focused Chromium checks pass explicit fallback priority, fallback cross-axis alignment, height and width auto-sizing, trigger-width matching, pinned placement, default side flipping, RTL logical placement, viewport clamping, top-layer rendering, and zero page errors. The tree-shaken Popover slice is 20,124 raw / 6,148 gzip / 5,475 Brotli bytes for the custom element and 43,696 / 11,678 / 10,305 for the React adapter (React externalized). Configurable collision boundaries and positioning strategy remain open.

Popover, Tooltip, Menu, and context-menu geometry checks pass; the menu parity verifier also passes after correcting its test fixture's undefined `defaultOpen` reference and asserting the boolean `checked` attribute semantically instead of requiring a specific serialized value. The consolidated Combobox/Virtualizer suite passes after the dynamic size cache and RTL variable-width end-anchor correction: initial visible-size callback count is 15 and rises to 25 after a distant scroll in the 100,000-item test. The refreshed Fluent 9.75.0 audits still show 0 missing named exports across 51 families and 0 own-prop name gaps across 227 contracts; runtime behavior parity remains in progress. Current SPFx 1.24 package checkpoint is a 200,980-byte `.sppkg` and an 855,473 raw / 195,841 gzip / 148,712 Brotli-byte demo bundle, cumulative and local only. Heft reports zero Jest suites; no tenant deployment or assistive-technology session was run.

### Fluent Field render-function controls and validation announcements — 2026-11-01

Field now accepts either a React control element or Fluent-style render-function child. ### Fluent Popover collision boundaries and positioning strategy — 2026-11-01

Popover positioning now accepts `flipBoundary` and `overflowBoundary` as `Element`, `DOMRectReadOnly`, or `null`, independently controlling fallback selection and final clamp/auto-size bounds. It also accepts Fluent's `positionFixed`; omitted/false uses absolute positioning and true uses fixed positioning. React and framework-neutral consumers share the same `.positioning` options, and the type is exported from both public entry points. Chromium verifies an element-based flip boundary, a separate overflow boundary, both CSS strategies, existing viewport/RTL collision handling, top-layer rendering, and zero page errors. Popover slices are 20,586 raw / 6,304 gzip / 5,630 Brotli bytes for the custom element and 44,191 / 11,845 / 10,438 for React with React externalized; compared with the prior measurement this adds 156 gzip bytes to the custom element and 167 to React. Tooltip, Menu, and context-menu geometry regressions pass. Catalog/parity audit and token drift check pass. No SPFx package rebuild or tenant verification was run. Fluent's positioning contract documents `flipBoundary`, `overflowBoundary`, and `positionFixed` in its positioning options [source](https://github.com/microsoft/fluentui/issues/26786).

Extended Popover `positioning.offset` from a number to Fluent's `{ mainAxis, crossAxis }` form while retaining numeric main-axis shorthand. `coverTarget` now aligns the surface over the anchor along the requested side. Both options run through the same geometry engine as the React prop and custom element. Chromium verifies main-axis distance, cross-axis displacement, cover placement, separate flip/overflow boundaries, absolute default and fixed mode, RTL/viewport collision regressions, and zero page errors. ### Fluent Virtualizer observer and scroll utilities — 2026-11-01

The React entry point now exports Fluent's observer/scroll helpers plus `useStaticVirtualizerMeasure`, `useDynamicVirtualizerMeasure`, and `useMeasureList`. Resize observers come from the element's owning window; intersection root margins refresh after a root's direction changes; static measurement computes viewport/buffer lengths; dynamic measurement uses measured prefix sizes to locate a visible range; indexed measurement preserves previously measured sizes while the collection grows or shrinks. Chromium verifies fixed/dynamic viewport sizing, context index updates at scroll position 240, indexed width measurement at 120px and resize to 31px height, visible intersections, LTR-to-RTL margin refresh, resize callback delivery, fixed/measured imperative scroll in both axes, and zero page errors. Legacy VirtualizerScrollView and axis verifiers pass. The combined nine-export utility slice is 6,121 raw / 2,295 gzip / 2,067 Brotli bytes (React externalized). Remaining package exports are Fluent's deprecated internal unstable render/style hooks and slot class-name constants; these are not equivalent to the component-level React API. See `scripts/verify-virtualizer-utilities.mjs` and `investigation/component-slice-benchmarks.json`.

### FileUpload form lifecycle — 2026-11-01

The first dedicated FileUpload verifier exposed two lifecycle defects. Reset cleared the selected files but retained the prior rejection message; reset now clears the live status. ### Theme and Tabs integration regression repair — local Chromium, 2026-11-01

The TabValue event includes its internal `valueType` discriminator so React can recover typed values across the custom-element event boundary; adding that field to emitted detail had left the shared React event-detail type stale and caused `tsc` to fail. The shared event type now declares the optional field. `verify:tabs-parity` and `verify:theme-ramps` pass after the repair. The theme suite verifies all 459 pinned Fluent values pass through a composed pack/provider, role defaults cover all 24 shared roles used across 67 source files, and the 60-case contrast matrix (light/dark browser schemes × five appearances × three brand colors × light/strong section surfaces) passes 300 text/control-state measurements at a minimum 4.56:1. `parity:audit` remains clean: 240 catalog entries, 227 own-prop contracts, and zero candidate callback-field gaps. The contrast matrix covers Link, visited-link role, Text, and primary Button rest/hover states; it does not prove contrast for every component/state. RichTextEditor’s focused verifier also passes native/React editing, sanitization, undo/redo, form lifecycle, RTL labels, and durable image upload. No new tokens were needed: `fluentTokens`, semantic packs, and arbitrary CSS custom properties already cover additive theme definition. Authenticated tenant, assistive-technology, and library-wide component/state contrast validation remain open.

Comparison against the installed `@fluentui/react-image@9.75.0` source found that non-default `fit` modes did not fill the image box when dimensions were omitted, `block` did not set full width, and `fit="none"` did not anchor the source image at top-left. ### Runtime-verification inventory update — 2026-11-01

The pinned reference was checked against npm and remains `@fluentui/react-components@9.75.0`, so the current API audit baseline is not stale. A simple filename scan of the 51 family catalog found families with no focused verifier; manual follow-up showed switch behavior is already covered by Checkbox and Field verifiers, InfoLabel by the Label verifier, and Image now has its own suite. Overflow was missing a focused runtime verifier and its catalog had no component examples, which kept the family name/prop audit from detecting gaps. Filename matching is only a discovery aid; it is not used as proof of missing or passing behavior.

Overflow’s `onOverflowChange` now matches Fluent’s `(null, { hasOverflow, itemVisibility, groupVisibility })` shape; native `spui-overflow-change` retains its visible/overflow ID arrays and adds those state fields. Mixed groups now report `overflow`, matching the installed Fluent priority manager. Horizontal and vertical axis measurement, `hasHiddenItems` menu reservation, and externally managed menu content are supported. `OverflowReorderObserver` is implemented as a renderless compatibility marker because the native engine already watches child-list mutations. `verify:overflow-parity` passes callback/event state, grouped visibility, both axes, external menu content, resize reflow, the marker’s no-DOM behavior, and zero browser errors; the example web part and README were migrated. SPFx 1.24 packaging passes at `.sppkg` 209,093 bytes; demo bundle 891,727 raw / 203,988 gzip / 154,834 Brotli. Overflow slices are 2,658 gzip bytes for the custom element and 16,222 for React (React externalized). See `scripts/verify-overflow-parity.mjs` and `investigation/component-slice-benchmarks.json`.

### Fluent grouped Carousel fade layout — local Chromium and SPFx 1.24 build, 2026-11-02

### Fluent Carousel slide attraction physics — local Chromium and SPFx library build, 2026-11-02

Fluent's `CarouselMotion` accepts a slide object with an attraction-duration coefficient (recommended 20–60), in addition to the string `slide` and `fade` modes. Added the typed object form and a dependency-free native scroll attraction loop using the same velocity/friction model; the React adapter forwards the coefficient as `motion-physics-duration`. A canceled smooth scroll could emit a late native `scrollend` and overwrite the requested page; scroll completion now ignores native events during the custom animation and commits its explicit requested target. `npm run verify:carousel-autoplay` passes the grouped drag source, interrupted-scroll path, object-duration reflection, RTL, reduced motion, fade, autoplay, and callback checks with zero browser errors. `npm run parity:check` validates all 243 catalog entries and reports zero named API, candidate prop/slot, or callback-field gaps; TypeScript build passes. ### Divider separator naming parity — pinned Fluent 9.75.0, local Chromium, 2026-11-02

The installed Fluent `useDividerBase_unstable` always exposes `role="separator"` and `aria-orientation`; when content exists it labels the separator through its wrapper. ### Responsive Image request ordering — local Chromium, 2026-11-02

The native `createImage` factory assigned `src` before `srcSet`, `sizes`, and intrinsic dimensions. Reordered assignment so responsive candidates and layout dimensions are ready before the request begins, reducing the chance of an unnecessary fallback fetch and layout shift. The Image browser verifier instruments native image property setters and confirms the order `srcset`, `sizes`, `width`, `height`, then `src`; existing React/native fit, dimensions, shape, border, shadow, alt, lazy-loading, and decoding checks pass with zero page errors. This proves property assignment order, not network selection or a measured latency/LCP gain across browsers.

### Tree checked-state and single-selection parity — Fluent 9.75.0 source and local Chromium, 2026-11-02

Source inspection of Fluent `react-tree` showed that activating a mixed item sets it checked, multiselect callback maps retain an explicit `false` value after unchecking, and single selection can be cleared by activating the selected item. The local adapter had treated mixed as selected-but-toggle-off, deleted unchecked map entries, and always selected in single mode; its default mixed state also had no state map to update after interaction. React Tree now initializes and updates uncontrolled checked state while preserving `true`/`false`/`mixed`, computes toggles from the checked map, and emits the same single-mode behavior. The custom-element Tree now accepts checked keys or `[key, true|false|mixed]` entries, renders tri-state checked values, includes the updated checked map in selection events, and toggles a selected item off in single mode. ### RichTextEditor pre-connection value rehydration — local Chromium, 2026-11-02

The framework-neutral editor silently discarded `.value` assignments made after `document.createElement()` but before insertion, a common data-hydration pattern for custom elements. ### Overflow reorder observation and first-event delivery — local Chromium, 2026-11-02

Confirmed Fluent's `OverflowReorderObserver` is a renderless `MutationObserver` on the overflow container's child list; the local custom-element engine already observes subtree child-list changes, so the React export intentionally renders nothing. Added a regression that reorders equal-priority items and confirms the native engine recalculates visibility without the marker. That surfaced a real React event race: the engine's first animation-frame update could fire before the passive `useSpuiEvent` subscription was attached, leaving React overflow state and callbacks empty. The shared event hook now subscribes in a layout effect and keeps a stable listener while updating its callback ref, avoiding repeated remove/add churn on rerenders. `verify:overflow-parity` passes first-event delivery, dynamic reorder, React/native state, axis behavior, and zero page errors; `verify:form-controls` and full `parity:check` also pass. The use of a layout effect addresses this custom-element render timing; this slice did not measure a user-perceived performance delta or validate server rendering.
