### Fluent Tree, Virtualizer, and RadioGroup contracts — local Chromium and TypeScript, 2026-11-02

Follow-up declaration reviews found three concrete gaps. Tree now defaults to `selectionMode="none"` in React and native custom elements; explicit single and multiselect behavior remains covered. `VirtualizerDataRef` now has Fluent's `progressiveSizes`, `nodeSizes`, `setFlaggedIndex`, and `currentIndex` fields, while the existing `imperativeRef` remains the separate scroll handle. The size arrays are materialized only when `imperativeVirtualizerRef` is supplied, as the public ref requires complete arrays; ordinary Virtualizer use avoids this O(n) work. RadioGroup's callback now exposes `React.FormEvent<HTMLDivElement>` and supplies an event object with a real wrapper `div` currentTarget, the native input as target/nativeEvent, and React-style default-prevention, propagation, and persistence methods. Its runtime fixture asserts those fields and methods. Tree, RadioGroup, Virtualizer axis, utility, scroll-view, and Fluent-ref verifiers pass. The full static parity gate reports 243 catalog entries, 51/51 named families, 231 comparable prop contracts, and no candidate prop/slot/literal/callback-field gaps. These are declaration and targeted Chromium checks, not complete runtime, accessibility, or performance parity; tenant rendering and assistive-technology coverage remain open.

The full 51-family axe fixture was rerun after the Tree default change: zero violations and zero page errors. Axe-incomplete results remain on three shadow-root `aria-controls` references, one Dialog close-name check, and shadow-tree contrast; direct same-root ARIA-ID checks and explicit close-button naming checks cover those cases, while remaining axe-incomplete color targets are measured separately. Authenticated profile validation was attempted on Lab 059. The read-only preflight stopped before page creation or writes because the current package's web-part component is absent from `GetClientSideWebParts`; both tenant and site catalogs say 1.0.0.160 deployed/enabled/valid but `InstalledVersion` is blank, the app instance is still Initialized (9), and the published page serves older bundle `library-demo-web-part_6270481ab3bb269ebb1e.js` rather than current local bundle `library-demo-web-part_a2fea9fef7e2466510f3.js`. The read-only diagnostic confirms the page itself renders the older library (283 custom-element instances) with no console errors. Deployment guidance forbids another install retry while this instance remains status 9 after the prior guarded retry; current-source tenant validation is therefore unverified pending inspection of the SharePoint ALM job.

RadioGroup's React event bridge now carries the source input event, and the callback fixture verifies Fluent's `target`, wrapper `currentTarget`, `nativeEvent`, and event methods. The 51-family axe fixture remains at zero violations; `parity:check` passes; and SPFx 1.24 production build/package-solution succeeds, producing local bundle `library-demo-web-part_4976fd44e9453eb9fab8.js`. This package remains a local artifact: tenant catalogs still point at the existing .160 package and Lab 059 still serves the older bundle until the stuck ALM instance is resolved.

### SpinButton callback type parity — Fluent 9.75.0 declarations and local Chromium, 2026-11-02

Review of the pinned Fluent SpinButton declaration found its `onChange` payload allows an omitted `value`, and the callback must replace the inherited React DOM `onChange` type. Local `SpinButtonProps` now omits the inherited handler, exposes Fluent-compatible `SpinButtonOnChangeData`, and keeps the existing event union. The existing SpinButton verifier now compiles a usage fixture that checks the callback is accepted and `data.value` is optional, then exercises the same runtime interaction/form cases. Library build and `verify-spin-button-parity` pass. This closes one inherited-prop contract gap that the own-prop report cannot detect; inherited event types for the remaining adapters still require direct declaration review.

Slider shares the inherited React `onChange` collision found in SpinButton. Slider now omits that DOM prop and exports Fluent-compatible `SliderOnChangeData`; both focused verifiers compile callback examples before exercising interaction and form behavior. The refreshed own-prop audit remains 231 compared with zero declared prop, slot, or literal candidates. The audit excludes inherited event props, so direct type fixtures remain necessary for these callback contracts.

A callback-signature detector was added to the Fluent callback audit after these issues showed that its payload-field check did not see inherited React handler intersections. The detector scans all 64 catalogued callbacks and fails parity checks when a Fluent multi-argument callback also exposes a conflicting local one-argument handler. It first found Slider, AlphaSlider, and ColorArea/ColorSlider alongside SpinButton; those types now omit inherited `onChange`, and type fixtures cover the corrected callback shapes. The callback audit and `--check` now report zero inherited-handler collisions, zero payload-field gaps, and zero payload-shape candidates. Library build and SpinButton, Slider, and ColorPicker verifiers pass; this detector covers arity collisions only, not callback event types or runtime semantics.

### React callback event contracts — Fluent 9.75.0 declarations and focused Chromium, 2026-11-02

Extended the callback audit to compare first-parameter event assignability and distinguish safe narrower local events from local types that cannot satisfy Fluent-typed callbacks. React `Dialog` now bridges its underlying native trigger/keyboard event into a React event whose currentTarget is the dialog surface, and a compile-time/runtime regression checks both. Added the shared `SharePointUIEvent` type and aligned callback signatures for ColorPicker/ColorArea/ColorSlider/AlphaSlider, List, Rating, SwatchPicker, TagPicker, TagGroup selection, and Carousel index change. SwatchPicker now supplies a React-shaped click event and button currentTarget in its React adapter while preserving native custom-event details. Build, Dialog, ColorPicker, and SwatchPicker verifiers pass. The updated callback report has zero payload-field gaps and inherited-handler collisions; it still identifies 13 unique event-contract candidates (15 rows, with DataGrid repeated for two family mappings), including Accordion, Card, DataGrid, Menu, Popover, TabList, TagGroup dismiss, Toolbar, Tooltip, and Tree. These declaration candidates need exact per-component event-flow review before resolution.


### Additional React callback event bridges — local Chromium, 2026-11-02

Aligned Menu checked-value, Card selection, Accordion toggle, and Popover open-change callbacks with React-shaped event contracts. Runtime review caught a source bug in Menu: its click listener referenced an implicit global `event`, which surfaced as `spui-select` instead of the original click; it now captures the actual click event. Card and Accordion event details now preserve their initiating event, and their React callbacks bridge it with the component host or source control as currentTarget. Popover similarly bridges native triggers and keeps programmatic changes representable. Added compile fixtures and extended focused Menu, Card, Accordion, and Popover verifiers; all four pass. The callback audit now reports 8 rows (6 unique remaining event contracts), with zero callback payload field gaps and zero inherited handler collisions. These are focused local runtime checks, not proof of full Fluent event behavior across all components.


### Tag dismiss and Tooltip visibility event contracts — local Chromium, 2026-11-02

Aligned TagGroup dismiss and Tooltip visibility callbacks with the corresponding Fluent React event types. Tag dismissal now carries its originating click through both the Tag and TagGroup custom events; Tooltip wraps pointer and focus sources as React events and retains the keyboard dismissal data with an undefined callback event. Runtime checks exposed and fixed the Tag dismiss button’s missing original-event detail. Compile fixtures and `verify:interaction-tag` / `verify:tooltip-parity` pass. The callback audit now reports 6 rows (4 unique event contracts: DataGrid resize and selection, plus Tree and FlatTree checked changes), with no payload-field or inherited-handler candidates.

### Tree event branch and Menu React event parity — local Chromium, 2026-11-02

Tree callback payloads now correlate each `type` discriminant with its mouse or keyboard event type, and a row click emits the Fluent `Click` navigation event. Menu React open-change callbacks now bridge mouse and keyboard source events while preserving native outside/scroll events in the payload. `verify:tree-parity`, `verify:tree-localization`, `verify:menu-parity`, the library build, and SPFx 1.24 production build pass. The refreshed static callback report covers 64 callbacks / 63 object payloads, with no missing payload fields, shape gaps, inherited-handler collisions, or aggregate event-type mismatches; it still lists 33 per-branch candidate mismatches across 14 callbacks and 12 local branch extensions. Those candidates require individual semantic review and do not by themselves establish runtime mismatches.

### Select native focus and tab-order parity — Fluent 9.75.0 Select contract and local Chromium, 2026-11-03

The Select React API already accepted native `autoFocus` and `tabIndex`, but forwarded them to the custom-element host while Fluent applies them to the primary native `<select>` slot. Select now forwards requested tab order to the internal or assigned native select, keeps the non-control host out of the tab sequence to avoid a duplicate stop, restores native defaults when `tabIndex` is removed, routes autofocus to the actual select, and delegates host `focus()`/`blur()` calls to that control. `verify:select-parity` now covers dynamic autofocus and `tabIndex` forwarding/restoration, and passes with zero page errors. This closes those focus-attribute gaps only; full Select visual, cross-browser, assistive-technology, theme, tenant, and performance parity remain open.

### Recursive external accessible names for shadow controls — local Chromium, WebKit, and SPFx 1.24, 2026-11-03

External `aria-labelledby` references are now resolved recursively, in IDREF order, with cycle protection and accessible-name precedence for nested references, `aria-label`, common host alternatives, embedded text-control values, and hidden referenced subtrees. Visible referenced subtrees omit hidden descendants. The resolver follows the recursive IDREF, hidden-target, and embedded-control rules in the [current W3C Accessible Name and Description Computation 1.2 Working Draft](https://www.w3.org/TR/2026/WD-accname-1.2-20261021/). The observer follows nested references, unresolved IDs, text/attribute/value changes, and target replacement, and releases its observer/listeners when the host disconnects. SplitButton now shares the same resolver for its primary and menu action names. `verify-form-associated-controls.mjs` passes the existing 18 React control / 11 family sweep plus nested chains, cycles, ordering, hidden references, changing embedded values, and replaced targets in Chromium and WebKit. `verify-button-parity.mjs` and the SPFx 1.24 production package build pass. This is not a complete AccName implementation: generated CSS text, every native host-language labeling rule, every ARIA role mapping, and assistive-technology output remain outside this increment and require separate evidence.

### External accessible descriptions across shadow controls — local Chromium, WebKit, and SPFx 1.24, 2026-11-03

External `aria-describedby` references previously reached an Input host but produced no accessible description on its inner native control. The shared resolver now follows nested description ID references and updates when targets change or are replaced; same-shadow-root references stay native. Field composition combines caller help text with Field hint and validation text, and its React and custom-element paths now mark that composition explicitly. `verify-form-associated-controls.mjs` passes the 18-control sweep and live recursive name/description updates in Chromium and WebKit; `verify-field-composition.mjs` passes the composed-description assertion for React and custom-element controls. The library and SPFx 1.24 production package build pass. Generated CSS descriptions, complete native host-language description rules, assistive-technology output, and broader Fluent behavioral parity remain outside this increment.

### Button and ComboBox external description parity across shadow controls — local Chromium, WebKit, and SPFx 1.24, 2026-11-03

Expanding the external `aria-describedby` regression from Input to all 11 form controls that use the shared accessible-name sync exposed two gaps. ComboBox's `syncAria()` called the shared cross-shadow resolver, then copied the external `aria-describedby` ID and host `aria-description` directly back onto its trigger; it now leaves descriptions to the shared resolver and continues to synchronize its separately managed `aria-invalid` state. Button's attribute callback also directly copied description attributes instead of re-running the shared resolver, which left stale text after the host reference was removed; it now delegates description changes to the helper. The form-control sweep passes in Chromium and WebKit for all 11 controls through both React and native custom-element APIs, including recursive references, live text changes, target replacement, `aria-describedby` precedence over `aria-description`, fallback restoration, and reference removal. `verify:combobox-compounds`, `verify:combobox-multiselect`, Button parity, Field composition, library build, and SPFx 1.24 production package build pass. This is focused runtime evidence for shared external descriptions; complete Fluent accessibility behavior and assistive-technology output remain open.

### Existing custom-element markup upgrade — local Chromium and WebKit, 2026-11-03

The expanded native fixture creates all 18 form-associated custom-element tags before the React adapter registers them, then allows definition/upgrade to run against already-connected markup. That exposed FileUpload's `attributeChangedCallback` calling `sync()` while the upgraded element's shadow input was still unset. It now waits for the input to exist; its `connectedCallback` renders and synchronizes the initial attributes after upgrade. The 18-tag native/React mount sweep has zero page errors in Chromium and WebKit, `verify:file-upload` passes accepted/rejected file handling, form data/validity/reset, disabled fieldsets, and focus restoration, and the SPFx 1.24 production package builds. This validates upgrade order and FileUpload's behavior, not every custom-element attribute combination or assistive-technology output.

### External labels and descriptions across all form controls — local Chromium, WebKit, and SPFx 1.24, 2026-11-03

Expanding the accessible-name/description fixture from the 11 shared-helper controls to all 18 form-associated React/native control pairs exposed missing external `aria-labelledby` forwarding in DatePicker, Rating, and TagPicker, and missing cross-shadow `aria-describedby` resolution in ColorPicker, FileUpload, and RichTextEditor. Those components now use the shared host accessible-name/description synchronizer on their primary controls, preserving their native invalid-state and label behavior. `verify-form-associated-controls.mjs` passes Chromium and WebKit for all 18 React/native pairs, including recursive descriptions and live target updates/replacement; full `parity:check` and SPFx 1.24 production packaging pass. The docs site builds 70 static pages. These are focused contract checks; complete Fluent behavior, screen-reader output, tenant rendering, and overall parity remain open.

### Generated theme-token inventory freshness — local static parity gate, 2026-11-03

The theme-token index had drifted from the source after Nav and Carousel began consuming shared accent, divider, and toggle-state variables. Regenerated `packages/sharepoint-ui/tokens.json` (397 indexed `--spui-*` variables) and added `tokens:check` to `parity:check` so future source/index divergence fails the standard parity gate. The independent theme fixture already exercises pass-through of all 459 Fluent v9 theme values; this slice keeps the library's own token-to-component inventory current, not a claim that every component's rendered styling matches Fluent.

### Button adapter mount overhead — local Chromium, 2026-11-03

The 500-button benchmark showed that the React Button adapter passed default `as="button"` and `icon-position="before"` plus many undefined optional attributes to every custom-element host. The adapter now omits those defaults and undefined values while preserving explicit props and context size. Shared `ensureDefined` also caches each successfully registered element definition, avoiding a custom-element registry lookup on every component render. The benchmark now compares Fluent, the normal React adapter, a raw React `spui-button` host, and direct custom-element insertion to isolate wrapper overhead. After five warmups and 20 measured rounds on Chromium 153, 100-button medians are 1.8 ms for both Fluent and the adapter; at 500, the adapter is 8.6 ms versus Fluent 7.7 ms for default theme and 8.8 ms versus 8.7 ms for the custom theme. Raw React hosts measure 7.4/7.5 ms, so about 1.2 ms of adapter overhead remains at 500. `verify:button-parity`, `parity:check`, and SPFx 1.24 packaging pass. This single local mount workload does not establish update, large-list, cross-browser, tenant, or user-perceived performance parity.
